\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{(\alpha,x)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{\ell}.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fork}}()~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{(\alpha,x)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{\ell}.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fork}}()~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{(\alpha,x)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{\ell}.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fork}}()~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{(\alpha,x)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{\ell}.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fork}}()~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C}\rrbracket_{L}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}let}}~{(\alpha,x)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fork}}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\alpha}}})~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}in}}~{\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}}&\text{if}~L=\ell\\
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}&\text{if}~L\neq\ell~\text{and}~\alpha,x\notin\operatorname{fv}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}})\\
\text{undefined}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L^{\prime}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{C}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L^{\prime}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{C}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L^{\prime}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{C}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L^{\prime}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{C}\rrbracket_{L}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\text{undefined}&\text{if}~L=L^{\prime}\\
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

D.3.
Endpoint Projection for Spawned Threads

We first define the function
\mathchoice{\text{tbodies}\left(C\right)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C)_{L}}
that collects the set of all bodies
C^{\prime}
of the
subterms in
C
of the form
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{C^{\prime}}
(that do not appear inside a closure).
Note that when a program has multiple subterms,
since a thread can only be spawned in one subterm (except for case-expressions),
the function does not union the
\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill-after}}}
s from the subterms together,
but rather uses the function
S_{1}\bowtie S_{2}

which selects the nonempty set among
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
,
or otherwise returns
\varnothing
if both are nonempty.

\displaystyle S_{1}\bowtie S_{2}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}S_{2}&S_{1}=\varnothing\\
S_{1}&S_{2}=\varnothing\\
\varnothing&S_{1}\neq\varnothing~\text{and}~S_{2}\neq\varnothing\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\text{tbodies}\left(X\right)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(X)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(X)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(X)_{L}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\text{tbodies}\left(\rho.e\right)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(\rho.e)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(\rho.e)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(\rho.e)_{L}}=\mathchoice{\text{tbodies}\left(\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fun}}}_{\rho}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C}\right)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fun}}}_{\rho}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C})_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fun}}}_{\rho}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C})_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fun}}}_{\rho}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C})_{L}}=\mathchoice{\text{tbodies}\left(\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}tfun}}}{F}({\alpha})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C}\right)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}tfun}}}{F}({\alpha})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C})_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}tfun}}}{F}({\alpha})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C})_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}tfun}}}{F}({\alpha})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C})_{L}}=\varnothing

{}\mid\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4844,0.1289}inr}~{y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}}\end{array})_{L}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\text{tbodies}\left(C\right)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C)_{L}}\bowtie(\mathchoice{\text{tbodies}\left(C_{1}\right)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C_{1})_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C_{1})_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C_{1})_{L}}\cup\mathchoice{\text{tbodies}\left(C_{2}\right)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C_{2})_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C_{2})_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C_{2})_{L}})

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\text{tbodies}\left(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L^{\prime}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{C}\right)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L^{\prime}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{C})_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L^{\prime}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{C})_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L^{\prime}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{C})_{L}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\{C\}&\text{if}~L=L^{\prime}\\
\mathchoice{\text{tbodies}\left(C\right)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C)_{L}}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Using this function we can then define
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}
to project non-threads normally,
and to project to the projected then merged
\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill-after}}}
bodies followed by an
\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}exit}}
for threads.

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}=\begin{cases}\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}&\text{if}~\mathchoice{\text{tbodies}\left(C\right)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C)_{L}}=\varnothing\\
(\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{n}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{n}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{n}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{n}\rrbracket_{L}})\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}exit}}&\text{if}~\mathchoice{\text{tbodies}\left(C\right)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C)_{L}}{\text{tbodies}(C)_{L}}=\{C_{1},\ldots,C_{n}\}~\text{where}\end{cases}

D.4.
Locations Named by a Type or Choreography

The set of named locations in a choreography (resp. type)
\operatorname{NL}({C})
(resp.
\operatorname{NL}({t})
) is defined as follows.

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\alpha})

\displaystyle=\varnothing

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({L})

\displaystyle=\{L\}

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\{\ell\}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\ell})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\top})

\displaystyle=\varnothing

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({t_{e}@\rho})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\rho}\tau_{2}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\tau_{1}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\tau_{2}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\tau_{1}+_{\rho}\tau_{2}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\tau_{1}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\tau_{2}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\tau_{1}\times\tau_{2}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\tau_{1}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\tau_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\mu_{\rho}\alpha\ldotp\tau})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\tau})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\forall{\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu\kappa}[{\rho}]\ldotp{\tau}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\tau})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({X})

\displaystyle=\varnothing

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\rho.e})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fun}}}_{\rho}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({C_{1}\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho}}C_{2}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}tfun}}}{F}({\alpha})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({C\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho}}\ell})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({C})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\ell})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({C\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho}}\rho^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({C})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({C\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho}}t})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({C})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fold}}_{\rho}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}unfold}}_{\rho}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({(C_{1},C_{2})_{\rho}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fst}}_{\rho}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}snd}}_{\rho}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}_{\rho}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}_{\rho}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}localCase}}_{\rho}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
{}\mid\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4844,0.1289}inl}~{x}\Rightarrow{C_{1}}\\
{}\mid\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4844,0.1289}inr}~{y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.\alpha}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({C\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\{}\ell{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\}}$}\mkern-2.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\rightsquigarrow}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\{}\ell{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\}}$}\mkern-2.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\rightsquigarrow}}{\mkern 3.0mu\raisebox{0.38751pt}{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\{}\ell{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\}}$}\mkern-1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\rightsquigarrow}}{\mkern 3.0mu\raisebox{0.19376pt}{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\{}\ell{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\}}$}\mkern-1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\rightsquigarrow}}}\rho})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\ell})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({{\ell}{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}[}{d}{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}]}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\rightsquigarrow}}{\rho}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641};}}C})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\ell})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{(\alpha,x)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{\ell}.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fork}}()~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{NL}({\ell})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\{L\}\cup\operatorname{NL}({C})

The set of named locations
\operatorname{NL}^{\top}({C})
including
\top
has
an identical definition, except that
\operatorname{NL}^{\top}({\top})=\top
,
rather than
\operatorname{NL}({\top})=\varnothing
.

D.5.
Spawned Locations in a Choreography

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({X})

\displaystyle=\varnothing

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({\rho.e})

\displaystyle=\varnothing

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fun}}}_{\rho}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho}}C_{2}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}tfun}}}{F}({\alpha})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({C\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho}}t})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fold}}_{\rho}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}unfold}}_{\rho}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({(C_{1},C_{2})_{\rho}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fst}}_{\rho}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}snd}}_{\rho}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}_{\rho}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}_{\rho}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SN}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C})\cup\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SN}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}localCase}}_{\rho}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
{}\mid\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4844,0.1289}inl}~{x}\Rightarrow{C_{1}}\\
{}\mid\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4844,0.1289}inr}~{y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C})\cup\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.\alpha}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({C\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\{}\ell{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\}}$}\mkern-2.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\rightsquigarrow}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\{}\ell{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\}}$}\mkern-2.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\rightsquigarrow}}{\mkern 3.0mu\raisebox{0.38751pt}{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\{}\ell{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\}}$}\mkern-1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\rightsquigarrow}}{\mkern 3.0mu\raisebox{0.19376pt}{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\{}\ell{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\}}$}\mkern-1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\rightsquigarrow}}}\rho})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({{\ell}{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}[}{d}{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}]}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\rightsquigarrow}}{\rho}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641};}}C})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{(\alpha,x)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{\ell}.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fork}}()~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{SL}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\{L\}\cup\operatorname{SL}({C})

D.6.
The Less-Than Relation

D.7.
The Simulating Less-Than Relation

The
simulating less-than relation

\precsim
is a subrelation of
\preceq
which differs in the following ways:

(1)

it does not contain a rule to allow
E_{1}\preceq V\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2}
,

(2)

corresponding expressions in evaluation position must be related by
\precsim
,
but corresponding continuations may be related by
\preceq
, and

(3)

the rules for function applications and pairs differ in how their right-hand arguments must be related
depending on whether their left-hand arguments are values.

This relation is so-named because if
E_{1}\precsim E_{2}
,
then the next step that
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
make—if any—must be identical
(i.e.,
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
simulate each other for a single step),
whereas this is not the case for
\preceq
.

\frac{~}{\text{undefined}\precsim E}\frac{~}{X\precsim X}\frac{~}{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}\precsim\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}}\frac{~}{X\precsim\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}}\frac{~}{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}\precsim X}\frac{~}{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e})\precsim\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e})}\frac{E_{1,1}\precsim E_{2,1}\quad E_{1,2}\preceq E_{2}}{\operatorname{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fun}}}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{E_{1}}\precsim\operatorname{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fun}}}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{E_{2}}}\frac{\neg\operatorname{Val}({E_{1,1}})\quad E_{1,1}\precsim E_{2,1}\quad E_{1,2}\preceq E_{2,2}}{E_{1,1}~E_{1,2}\precsim E_{2,1}~E_{2,2}}\frac{\operatorname{Val}({E_{1,1}})\quad E_{1,1}\precsim E_{2,1}\quad E_{1,2}\precsim Appendix E
Proofs

E.1.
Substitution Lemmas

The following lemmas quantify the behavior of the kinding and type systems with respect to the substitution operations.
Each lemma is proven with respect to an infinite parallel substitution
\sigma
mapping all variables to choreographies (or types, or local expressions),
of which a single-variable substitution
[X\mapsto C]
can be recovered as a special case by setting
\sigma(X)=C
and
\sigma(Y)=Y
for
Y\neq X
.
We make use of these lemmas frequently, and so may elide explicitly referencing them in any following proofs.

Lemma 1 (Location Substitution Preserves Containment).

If
\ell\in\rho
then
\ell[\sigma]\in\rho[\sigma]
.

Proof.

By induction on
\rho
.
∎

Lemma 2 (Location Substitution Preserves Subsets).

If
\rho_{1}\subseteq\rho_{2}
then
\rho_{1}[\sigma]\subseteq\rho_{2}[\sigma]
.

Proof.

By induction on the definition of the
\subseteq
relation.
The only interesting case is when
\rho_{1}=\{\ell\}
, which follows by Lemma
1
.
∎

Definition 1.

For two location sets
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
, say that
\rho_{1}\equiv\rho_{2}

if and only if
\rho_{1}\subseteq\rho_{2}
and
\rho_{2}\subseteq\rho_{1}
,
where
\subseteq
is as defined in Appendix
A.7
.

Lemma 3.

For any location substitution
\sigma
,
\operatorname{NL}({t[\sigma]})\equiv\operatorname{NL}({t})\cup\bigcup_{\alpha\in\operatorname{fv}({t})}\operatorname{NL}({\sigma(\alpha)})
.

Proof.

By induction on
t
.
∎

Lemma 4.

For any type substitution
\sigma
,
\operatorname{NL}({t[\sigma]})\equiv\operatorname{NL}({t})\cup\bigcup_{\alpha\in\operatorname{fv}({t})}\operatorname{NL}({\sigma(\alpha)})
.

Proof.

By induction on
t
.
∎

Lemma 5.

For any location substitution
\sigma
,
\operatorname{SL}({C[\sigma]})=\operatorname{SL}({C})
.

Proof.

By induction on
C
.
∎

Lemma 6.

For any type substitution
\sigma
,
\operatorname{SL}({C[\sigma]})=\operatorname{SL}({C})
.

Proof.

By induction on
C
.
∎

Lemma 7.

For any local substitution
\sigma
,
\operatorname{SL}({C[\rho|\sigma]})=\operatorname{SL}({C})
.

Proof.

By induction on
C
.
∎

Lemma 8.

For any choreographic substitution
\sigma
,
\operatorname{SL}({C[\sigma]})\equiv\operatorname{SL}({C})\cup\bigcup_{X\in\operatorname{fv}({C})}\operatorname{SL}({\sigma(X)})
.

Proof.

By induction on
C
.
∎

Definition 2 (Well-formed Location-Type Substitutions).

Say that a function
\sigma
from location-type variables to locations or location sets
maps
\Gamma_{\ell,1}
to
\Gamma_{\ell,2}
(written
\,\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\sigma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\Gamma_{\ell,1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\Rightarrow}}\Gamma_{\ell,2}
) if and only if

\forall\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu\kappa_{\ell}\in\Gamma_{\ell,1}\ldotp\Gamma_{\ell,2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\sigma(\alpha)\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}\kappa_{\ell}.

Lemma 9 (Location Substitution Preserves Location Kinding).

If
\,\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\sigma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\Gamma_{\ell,1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\Rightarrow}}\Gamma_{\ell,2}
and

\Gamma_{\ell,1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}t\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}\kappa_{\ell}
,
then
\Gamma_{\ell,2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}t[\sigma]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}\kappa_{\ell}
.

Proof.

By induction on the kinding derivation
\Gamma_{\ell,1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}t\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}\kappa_{\ell}
.
∎

Lemma 10 (Location Substitution Preserves Kinding).

If
\,\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\sigma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\Gamma_{\ell,1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\Rightarrow}}\Gamma_{\ell,2}
and
\Gamma_{\ell,1};\Gamma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}t\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}\kappa
,
then
\Gamma_{\ell,2};\Gamma[\sigma]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}t[\sigma]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}\kappa[\sigma]
.

Proof.

By induction on the kinding derivation
\Gamma_{\ell,1};\Gamma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}t\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}\kappa
.
∎

Definition 3.

For a location substitution
\sigma
and a set of locations
\Omega
, say that
\sigma
does not mention
\Omega

(written
\Omega\notin\sigma
) if and only if

L\neq\sigma(\alpha)
and
L\notin\sigma(\alpha)
for all location-type variables
\alpha
and
L\in\Omega
.

Lemma 11 (Unmentioned Substitutions Preserve Equality).

If
\Omega\notin\sigma
, then for all
L\in\Omega
,
\ell=L
if and only if
\ell[\sigma]=L
.

Lemma 12 (Unmentioned Substitutions Preserve Containment).

If
\Omega\notin\sigma
, then for all
L\in\Omega
,
L\in\rho
if and only if
L\in\rho[\sigma]
.

Lemma 13 (Unmentioned Substitutions Preserve Containment in Named Locations).

If
\Omega\notin\sigma
, then for all
L\in\Omega
,
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho})
if and only if
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho[\sigma]})
.

Lemma 14 (Unmentioned Substitutions Preserve Disjointness).

If
\Omega\notin\sigma
, then
\Omega\cap\rho=\varnothing
if and only if
\Omega\cap\rho[\sigma]=\varnothing
.

Lemma 15 (Unmentioned Substitutions Preserve Disjointness in Named Locations).

If
\Omega\notin\sigma
, then
\Omega\cap\operatorname{NL}({\rho})=\varnothing
if and only if
\Omega\cap\operatorname{NL}({\rho[\sigma]})=\varnothing
.

Lemma 16 (Context Projection and Location Substitution Commute).

If
\sigma
is a location substitution,
\Delta_{e}
is a local context, and
\rho
is a location set, then

(\left.\Delta_{e}\right|_{\rho})[\sigma]\subseteq\left.\Delta_{e}[\sigma]\right|_{\rho[\sigma]}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Delta_{e}
.
If
\Delta_{e}=\cdot
, the claim is trivial.
Otherwise suppose that
\Delta_{e}=\rho^{\prime}.x\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mut_{e},\Delta_{e}^{\prime}
.
If
\rho\subseteq\rho^{\prime}
, then
\left.\Delta_{e}\right|_{\rho}=x\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mut_{e},\left.\Delta_{e}^{\prime}\right|_{\rho}
,
and so

(\left.\Delta_{e}\right|_{\rho})[\sigma]=x\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mut_{e}[\sigma],(\left.\Delta_{e}^{\prime}\right|_{\rho})[\sigma]\subseteq x\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mut_{e}[\sigma],\left.\Delta_{e}^{\prime}[\sigma]\right|_{\rho[\sigma]}

by induction.
By Lemma
2
,
\rho[\sigma]\subseteq\rho^{\prime}[\sigma]
, so

\left.\Delta_{e}[\sigma]\right|_{\rho[\sigma]}=\left.(\rho^{\prime}[\sigma].x\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mut_{e}[\sigma],\Delta_{e}^{\prime}[\sigma])\right|_{\rho[\sigma]}=x\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mut_{e}[\sigma],\left.\Delta_{e}^{\prime}[\sigma]\right|_{\rho[\sigma]}

as desired.
Otherwise suppose that
\rho\not\subseteq\rho^{\prime}
.
In this case,

(\left.\Delta_{e}\right|_{\rho})[\sigma]=(\left.\Delta_{e}^{\prime}\right|_{\rho})[\sigma]\subseteq\left.\Delta_{e}^{\prime}[\sigma]\right|_{\rho[\sigma]}.

We could have either
\rho[\sigma]\subseteq\rho^{\prime}[\sigma]
—in which case
\left.\Delta_{e}[\sigma]\right|_{\rho[\sigma]}=x\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mut_{e}[\sigma],\left.\Delta_{e}^{\prime}[\sigma]\right|_{\rho[\sigma]}
as before—or

\rho[\sigma]\not\subseteq\rho^{\prime}[\sigma]
, wherein
\left.\Delta_{e}[\sigma]\right|_{\rho[\sigma]}=\left.\Delta_{e}^{\prime}[\sigma]\right|_{\rho[\sigma]}
.
In either instance,
(\left.\Delta_{e}\right|_{\rho})[\sigma]\subseteq\left.\Delta_{e}[\sigma]\right|_{\rho[\sigma]}
, completing the proof.
∎

Lemma 17 (Location Substitution Preserves Augmented Typing).

If
\,\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\sigma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\Gamma_{\ell,1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\Rightarrow}}\Gamma_{\ell,2}
,

\Gamma_{\ell,1};\Gamma;\Delta_{e};\Delta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,
and
\operatorname{SL}({C})\notin\sigma
,
then
\Gamma_{\ell,2};\Gamma[\sigma];\Delta_{e}[\sigma];\Delta[\sigma]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C[\sigma]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau[\sigma]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho[\sigma]
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation
\Gamma_{\ell,1};\Gamma;\Delta_{e};\Delta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
.
In the following we denote
\Theta_{1}=\Gamma_{\ell,1};\Gamma;\Delta_{e};\Delta
and

\Theta_{2}=\Gamma_{\ell,2};\Gamma[\sigma];\Delta_{e}[\sigma];\Delta[\sigma]
for simplicity.

as desired.

.

as desired.

Corollary 1.

If
\Theta,\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu\kappa_{\ell}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}t\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}\kappa_{\ell}
, and
t\notin\operatorname{SL}({C})
, then
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}\mathchoice{C{\left[\alpha\mapsto t\right]}}{C[\alpha\mapsto t]}{C[\alpha\mapsto t]}{C[\alpha\mapsto t]}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\mathchoice{\tau{\left[\alpha\mapsto t\right]}}{\tau[\alpha\mapsto t]}{\tau[\alpha\mapsto t]}{\tau[\alpha\mapsto t]}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\mathchoice{\rho{\left[\alpha\mapsto t\right]}}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto t]}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto t]}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto t]}
.

Definition 4 (Well-formed Type Substitutions).

Say that a function
\sigma
from type variables to types
maps
\Gamma_{1}
to
\Gamma_{2}
under
\Gamma_{\ell}
(written
\Gamma_{\ell}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\sigma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\Gamma_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\Rightarrow}}\Gamma_{2}
) if and only if

\forall\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu\kappa\in\Gamma_{1}\ldotp\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\sigma(\alpha)\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}\kappa.

Lemma 18 (Type Substitution Preserves Kinding).

If
\Gamma_{\ell}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\sigma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\Gamma_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\Rightarrow}}\Gamma_{2}
,

\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}t\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}\kappa
, and

\Gamma_{\ell}\vdash\Gamma_{2}
,
then
\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}t[\sigma]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}\kappa[\sigma]
.

Proof.

By induction on the kinding derivation
\Gamma_{\ell},\Gamma_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}t\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}\kappa
,
and using the fact that the local kinding system is preserved under well-formed type substitutions.
∎

Lemma 19 (Context Projection and Type Substitution Commute).

If
\sigma
is a type substitution,
\Delta_{e}
is a local context, and
\rho
is a location set, then

(\left.\Delta_{e}\right|_{\rho})[\sigma]=\left.\Delta_{e}[\sigma]\right|_{\rho}
.

Proof.

The proof is identical to Lemma
16
, also noting that type substitution does not affect location sets so
that both projected contexts contain the same variables.
∎

Lemma 20 (Type Substitution Preserves Augmented Typing).

If
\Gamma_{\ell}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\sigma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\Gamma_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\Rightarrow}}\Gamma_{2}
,

\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma_{1};\Delta_{e};\Delta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
, and

\Gamma_{\ell}\vdash\Gamma_{2}
,
then
\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma_{2};\Delta_{e}[\sigma];\Delta[\sigma]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C[\sigma]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau[\sigma]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
.

Proof.

The argument proceeds similarly to Lemma
17
,
also using the assumption that the local type system is preserved under well-formed type substitutions,
and that locations and location sets are unaffected by type substitution.
∎

Corollary 2.

If
\Theta,\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu\kappa\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
and
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}t\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}\kappa
, then
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}\mathchoice{C{\left[\alpha\mapsto t\right]}}{C[\alpha\mapsto t]}{C[\alpha\mapsto t]}{C[\alpha\mapsto t]}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\mathchoice{\tau{\left[\alpha\mapsto t\right]}}{\tau[\alpha\mapsto t]}{\tau[\alpha\mapsto t]}{\tau[\alpha\mapsto t]}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
.

Definition 5 (Well-formed Local Substitutions).

Say that a function
\sigma
from local variables to local expressions
maps
\Delta_{e,1}
to
\Delta_{e,2}
under
\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma
(written
\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\sigma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\Delta_{e,1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\Rightarrow}}\Delta_{e,2}
) if and only if

\forall\rho.x\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mut_{e}\in\Delta_{e,1}\ldotp\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma;\left.\Delta_{e,2}\right|_{\rho}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.4844,0.1289}\Vdash}\sigma(x)\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.4844,0.1289}:}t_{e}.

Lemma 21 (Local Substitution Preserves Augmented Typing).

If
\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\sigma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\Delta_{e,1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\Rightarrow}}\Delta_{e,2}
,

\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma;\Delta_{e,1};\Delta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
, and

\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma\vdash\Delta_{e,2}
,
then
\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma;\Delta_{e,2};\Delta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C[\sigma]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation
\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma;\Delta_{e,1};\Delta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,
and using the fact that the local type system is preserved under well-formed local substitutions.
∎

Corollary 3.

If
\Theta,\rho^{\prime}.x\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mut_{e}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
and

\left.\Theta\right|_{\rho^{\prime}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.4844,0.1289}\Vdash}e\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.4844,0.1289}:}t_{e}
,
then
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}\mathchoice{C{\left[\rho^{\prime}|x\mapsto e\right]}}{C[\rho^{\prime}|x\mapsto e]}{C[\rho^{\prime}|x\mapsto e]}{C[\rho^{\prime}|x\mapsto e]}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
.

Lemma 22 (Well-Typed Programs Have No Spawned Locations).

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
, then
\operatorname{SL}({C})=\varnothing
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation, noting that
\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill-after}}}
is not well-typed.
∎

Definition 6 (Well-formed Substitutions).

Say that a function
\sigma
from program variables to choreographies
maps
\Delta_{1}
to
\Delta_{2}
under
\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma;\Delta_{e}

(written
\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma;\Delta_{e}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\sigma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\Delta_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\Rightarrow}}\Delta_{2}
) if and only if

\forall X\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mu\tau\in\Delta_{1}\ldotp\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma;\Delta_{e};\Delta_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\sigma(X)\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\varnothing.

Lemma 23 (Substitution Preserves Augmented Typing).

If
\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma;\Delta_{e}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\sigma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\Delta_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\Rightarrow}}\Delta_{2}
,

\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma;\Delta_{e};\Delta_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
, and

\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma\vdash\Delta_{2}
,
then
\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma;\Delta_{e};\Delta_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C[\sigma]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation
\Gamma_{\ell};\Gamma;\Delta_{e};\Delta_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
.
The argument proceeds similarly to Lemma
17
,
and the only interesting cases are for variables.
Indeed, if
X\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mu\tau\in\Delta_{1}
, then as
\sigma
is well-formed,
\Theta_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}\sigma(X)\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\varnothing
.
This suffices because the premise is that
\Theta_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}X\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\varnothing
.
As well, Lemmas
22
and
8
give that the spawned locations in
C
(and all subterms) are not modified,
so all sets of locations that are disjoint by assumption remain disjoint after the substitution.
∎

Lemma 24 (Participants of Values).

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}V\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
and
\operatorname{Val}({V})
or
V=X
, then

\rho=\varnothing
and
\operatorname{SL}({V})=\varnothing
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}V\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
, noting that no introduction
form adds more locations to
\rho
than are in its subterms.
∎

Corollary 4.

If
\Theta,X\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mu\tau_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{2}
,
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}V\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{1}
, and
\operatorname{Val}({V})
,
then
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}\mathchoice{C{\left[X\mapsto V\right]}}{C[X\mapsto V]}{C[X\mapsto V]}{C[X\mapsto V]}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{2}
.

Lemma 25 (Location Set Subkinding).

If
\Gamma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\rho\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}*_{\mathsf{finlocset}}
, then
\Gamma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\rho\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}*_{\mathsf{locset}}
.

Proof.

Straightforward by induction on the kinding derivation
\Gamma\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\rho\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}*_{\mathsf{finlocset}}
.
The only interesting case is when
\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu*_{\mathsf{finlocset}}\in\Gamma
is a type variable,
for which the rule
K-SubVar
(
??
) suffices.
∎

E.2.
Type Soundness

Lemma 26.

For any concrete set
\Omega\subset\mathcal{L}
of locations,

\mathchoice{\rho{\left[\top\mapsto\Omega\right]}}{\rho[\top\mapsto\Omega]}{\rho[\top\mapsto\Omega]}{\rho[\top\mapsto\Omega]}\subseteq\Omega
if and only if

\mathchoice{\rho{\left[\top\mapsto\varnothing\right]}}{\rho[\top\mapsto\varnothing]}{\rho[\top\mapsto\varnothing]}{\rho[\top\mapsto\varnothing]}\subseteq\Omega
.

Proof.

By induction on
\rho
.
If
\rho=\varnothing
or
\rho=\top
, both sides are always true.
If
\rho=\alpha
or
\rho=\{\alpha\}
, both sides are always false.
If
\rho=\{L\}
, both sides are true if and only if
L\in\Omega
.
The case when
\rho=\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2}
follows by induction because substitution directly distributes over unions.
∎

Lemma 27.

For any type
t
,
\operatorname{NL}^{\top}({t})\setminus\top\equiv\operatorname{NL}({t})
.

Proof.

By induction on
t
.
∎

Lemma 28.

For any choreography
C
,
\operatorname{NL}^{\top}({C})\setminus\top\equiv\operatorname{NL}({C})
.

Proof.

By induction on
C
.
∎

Lemma 29.

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
then
\operatorname{SL}({C})\subseteq\operatorname{NL}({\rho})

and
\operatorname{NL}^{\top}({\rho})\subseteq\operatorname{NL}^{\top}({C})
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing judgment
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
.
∎

Lemma 30.

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
then
\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\operatorname{NL}({C})
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
.
The only interesting case is when
C=\rho.e
, wherein if
\operatorname{Val}({e})
we have
that
\varnothing\subseteq\operatorname{NL}({\rho})
, and otherwise
\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\operatorname{NL}({\rho})
.
∎

Lemma 31.

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
, then
\rho\subseteq\operatorname{cloc}({C})
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
.
The only interesting case is when
C=\rho.e
, wherein if
\operatorname{Val}({e})
we have
that
\varnothing\subseteq\rho
, and otherwise
\rho\subseteq\rho
.
∎

Lemma 32 (Single-Step Type Preservation).

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,

\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega
, and

\langle C,\Omega\rangle\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}\langle C^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime}\rangle
,
then there is some
\rho^{\prime}
such that all of the following properties hold.

(1)

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho^{\prime}

(2)

\operatorname{NL}({\rho^{\prime}})\subseteq\Omega^{\prime}

(3)

\operatorname{SL}({C^{\prime}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C})=\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega

(4)

\operatorname{SL}({C})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C^{\prime}})=\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\prime}

(5)

\operatorname{NL}({\rho^{\prime}})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega

(6)

\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\prime}\subseteq\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho^{\prime}})

Proof.

By induction on the step
\langle C,\Omega\rangle\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}\langle C^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime}\rangle
.
Most cases are immediate by induction and using the various substitution lemmas.

•

(
C-Ctx
(
??
))
We handle the case for reductions in pairs and
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}
.
The argument for the other cases, as well as the out-of-order cases, are similar.

For a pair
(C_{1},C_{2})
,
the assumptions are that
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{1}
,

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{2}
,

\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})
and
\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})
are disjoint,
and
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})
and
\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})
are disjoint.
First suppose the reduction is in the left-hand side.
By induction, there is some
\rho_{1}^{\prime}
where

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{1}^{\prime}
and conditions (2–6) hold.

(1)

First, we show that
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})
and
\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})
are disjoint.
Suppose that
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})
and
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})
.
By the assumption that
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})
and
\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})
are disjoint,
we must have that
L\notin\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})
.
Therefore
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})
, and hence

L\in\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega
by the inductive hypothesis.
But as
\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})\subseteq\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})\subseteq\Omega
, we have a contradiction, as desired.

Now we show that
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})
and
\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})
are disjoint.
Suppose that
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})
and
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})
.
By the assumption that
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})
and
\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})
are disjoint,
we must have that
L\notin\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})
.
Therefore
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})
, and hence

L\in\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega
by the inductive hypothesis.
But as
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})\subseteq\Omega
, we have a contradiction, as desired.

Thus by the argument above and by induction,
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}(C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2})_{\rho}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau_{1}\times\tau_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{1}^{\prime}\cup\rho_{2}
.

(2)

We show that
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})\subseteq\Omega^{\prime}
.
By induction
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})\subseteq\Omega^{\prime}
, so we need only show that
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})\subseteq\Omega^{\prime}
.
To that end, let
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})
, and suppose for contradiction that
L\notin\Omega^{\prime}
.
Then
L\in\Omega
because
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})\subseteq\Omega
by assumption, so
L\in\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\prime}
.
Then by the inductive hypothesis,
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})\subseteq\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})
.
But by assumption
\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})
and
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})
are disjoint, so we have a contradiction.

(3)

We need to show that
\operatorname{SL}({(C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2})_{\rho}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({(C_{1},C_{2})_{\rho}})=\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})\setminus(\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}}))=\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega
.
We can see that
\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})\setminus(\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}}))\subseteq\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega
easily because

\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\subseteq\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega
by the inductive hypothesis.
For the other direction, if
L\in\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega
, then
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})
, so we need only show that
L\notin\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})
.
This holds because if
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})\subseteq\operatorname{NL}({C_{2}})
, then we would have that
L\in\Omega
, a contradiction.

(4)

We need to show that
\operatorname{SL}({(C_{1},C_{2})_{\rho}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({(C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2})_{\rho}})=\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\setminus(\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})\cup\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}}))=\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\prime}
.
We can see that
\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\setminus(\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})\cup\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}}))\subseteq\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\prime}
easily because

\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})\subseteq\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\prime}
by the inductive hypothesis.
For the other direction, if
L\in\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\prime}
, then
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})
, so we need only show that
L\notin\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})
.
This holds because
L\in\operatorname{NL}({C_{1}})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})
by (6) of the induction, so
L\in\operatorname{NL}({C_{1}})
.
But then as
\operatorname{NL}({C_{1}})
and
\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}^{\prime}})
are disjoint,
L\notin\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})
as desired.

(5)

We need to show that
(\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}}))\setminus(\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}}))=\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})\setminus(\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}}))\subseteq\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega
.
However,
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})\subseteq\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega
by (5) of the inductive hypothesis, which is satisfactory.

(6)

We need to show that
\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\prime}\subseteq(\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}}))\setminus(\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}}))=\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})\setminus(\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}}))
.
To that end, let
L\in\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\prime}
.
Clearly
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})
as
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})\subseteq\Omega
, so we must show that
L\notin\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({C_{2}})
.
But by (6) of the inductive hypothesis,
L\notin\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})
, so we must simply show that
L\notin\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})
.
By (4) of the inductive hypothesis,
L\in\operatorname{SL}({\rho_{1}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})
, and hence
L\notin\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})
because
\operatorname{SL}({\rho_{1}})
and
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})
are disjoint.

The argument for reductions on the right-hand side of the pair is symmetric.

For
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}_{\rho}~{C}
,
the assumptions are that
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{1}
,

\operatorname{tloc}({\Theta};{\tau_{1}})\cup\operatorname{tloc}({\Theta};{\tau_{2}})\subseteq\rho
,

\operatorname{NL}({\rho})
and
\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})
are disjoint,
and
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega
.
By induction, there is some
\rho_{1}^{\prime}
where

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{1}^{\prime}
and conditions (2–6) hold.

(1)

We show that
\operatorname{NL}({\rho})
and
\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})
are disjoint.
Suppose that
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})
and
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho})
.
We must have that
L\notin\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})
, as
\operatorname{NL}({\rho})
and
\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})
are disjoint by assumption.
But then
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})=\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega
,
and hence
L\notin\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega
, a contradiction as desired.
Therefore by the argument above and by induction,
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}_{\rho}~{C_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau_{1}+_{\rho}\tau_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{1}^{\prime}
.

(2)

We need to show that
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})\subseteq\Omega^{\prime}
, which is precisely (2) of the inductive hypothesis.

(3)

We need to show that
\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}_{\rho}~{C_{1}^{\prime}}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}_{\rho}~{C_{1}}})=\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})=\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega
,
but this is precisely (3) of the inductive hypothesis.

(4)

Symmetrically,
\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}_{\rho}~{C_{1}}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}_{\rho}~{C_{1}^{\prime}}})=\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}^{\prime}})=\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\prime}

by (4) of the inductive hypothesis.

(5)

We need to show that
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})\subseteq\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega
, which is given directly by the inductive hypothesis.

(6)

Finally, we need to show that
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}^{\prime}})\subseteq\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\prime}
,
which is also provided by (6) of the inductive hypothesis.

Follows by local type preservation, and as no locations are spawned or killed.

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}\mathchoice{C_{2}{\left[\alpha\mapsto L\right]}}{C_{2}[\alpha\mapsto L]}{C_{2}[\alpha\mapsto L]}{C_{2}[\alpha\mapsto L]}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\mathchoice{\rho{\left[\alpha\mapsto L\right]}}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto L]}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto L]}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto L]}

\displaystyle\operatorname{NL}({\mathchoice{\rho{\left[\alpha\mapsto L\right]}}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto L]}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto L]}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto L]}})\setminus(\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}}))

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\mathchoice{\rho{\left[\alpha\mapsto L\right]}}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto L]}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto L]}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto L]}

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L^{\prime}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{C^{\prime}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\{L^{\prime}\}\cup\mathchoice{\rho{\left[\alpha\mapsto L\right]}}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto L]}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto L]}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto L]}

\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L^{\prime}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{C^{\prime}}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{(\alpha,x)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{L}.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fork}}()~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C}})=\{L^{\prime}\}=\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}V\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\varnothing

(2)

.

\operatorname{SL}({V})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{V}})=\varnothing\setminus\{L\}=\varnothing=\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega

The assumptions are that

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,

\{L\}\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega
, and

L\notin\operatorname{cloc}({C})
.

(1)

Clearly
C
is well-typed.

(2)

By assumption,
\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega
.
As well, by Lemma
31
we can say that

L\notin\operatorname{NL}({\rho})
, and so
\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega\setminus\{L\}=\Omega^{\prime}
.

(3)

\operatorname{SL}({C})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{C}})=\varnothing=\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega

(4)

\operatorname{SL}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{C}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C})=\{L\}=\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\prime}

(5)

\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\setminus(\{L\}\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho}))=\varnothing\subseteq\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega=\varnothing

(6)

(\{L\}\cup\operatorname{NL}({\rho}))\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho})=\{L\}\supseteq\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\prime}=\{L\}

∎

Theorem 8 (Full Preservation).

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,

\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega
, and

\langle C,\Omega\rangle\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{c}}\langle C^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime}\rangle
,
then there is some
\rho^{\prime}
such that all of the following properties hold.

(1)

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho^{\prime}

(2)

\operatorname{NL}({\rho^{\prime}})\subseteq\Omega^{\prime}

(3)

\operatorname{SL}({C^{\prime}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C})=\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega

(4)

\operatorname{SL}({C})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C^{\prime}})=\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\prime}

(5)

\operatorname{NL}({\rho^{\prime}})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega

(6)

\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\prime}\subseteq\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho^{\prime}})

Proof.

By induction on the length of the reduction sequence.
If the reduction is of length 0, the conclusion is immediate.
Otherwise suppose that
\langle C_{1},\Omega_{1}\rangle\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{c}}\langle C_{2},\Omega_{2}\rangle\mathrel{\Longrightarrow_{c}}\langle C_{3},\Omega_{3}\rangle
,
where we can apply the inductive hypothesis to the first reduction sequence, and then apply Theorem
32
to the last step.
(1–2) immediately hold by the conclusion of Theorem
32
.

(3)

We show that
\operatorname{SL}({C_{3}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\subseteq\Omega_{3}\setminus\Omega_{1}
, with the opposite direction following a symmetric argument.
Let
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C_{3}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})
.
In the case that
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})
, we have that
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})
, so
L\in\Omega_{2}\setminus\Omega_{1}
by (3) of the inductive hypothesis.
It must be the case that
L\in\Omega_{3}
, for otherwise
L\in\Omega_{2}\setminus\Omega_{3}
, which implies that
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C_{3}})
by (4) of Theorem
32
, a contradiction.
Therefore
L\in\Omega_{3}\setminus\Omega_{1}
as desired.
Now consider the case when
L\notin\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})
.
Then
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C_{3}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})
, so
L\in\Omega_{3}\setminus\Omega_{2}
by (3) of the last step.
It must be the case that
L\notin\Omega_{1}
, for otherwise
L\in\Omega_{1}\setminus\Omega_{2}
, which implies that
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C_{1}})\setminus\operatorname{SL}({C_{2}})
by (4) of the induction, a contradiction.
Therefore
L\in\Omega_{3}\setminus\Omega_{1}
as desired.

(4)

Symmetric to the argument for (3).

(5)

Suppose that
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{3}})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})
.
In the case that
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})
, then
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})
, so
L\in\Omega_{2}\setminus\Omega_{1}
by (5) of the induction.

L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{3}})
, so
L\in\Omega_{3}
by (2) of the last step, and hence
L\in\Omega_{3}\setminus\Omega_{1}
as desired.
Otherwise in the case that
L\notin\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})
, then
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{3}})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})
, so
L\in\Omega_{3}\setminus\Omega_{2}
by (5) of the last step.

L
cannot be in
\Omega_{1}
, for otherwise
L\in\Omega_{1}\setminus\Omega_{2}
, which by (6) of the inductive hypothesis would imply that
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})
, a contradiction.
Therefore
L\in\Omega_{3}\setminus\Omega_{1}
as (5) requires.

(6)

Suppose that
L\in\Omega_{1}\setminus\Omega_{3}
.
If
L\in\Omega_{2}
, by (6) of the last step we have
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{3}})
.
We must have that
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})
, for otherwise
L\in\Omega_{2}\setminus\Omega_{1}
by (5) of the induction, a contradiction.
Thus
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})
, and
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{3}})
, as desired.
Otherwise suppose that
L\notin\Omega_{2}
.
In this case,
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})
by (6) of the induction.
We must have that
L\notin\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{3}})
, for otherwise
L\in\Omega_{3}\setminus\Omega_{2}
by (5) of the last step, a contradiction.
Therefore
L\in\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{1}})\setminus\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{3}})
as (6) requires.

∎

Proof.

An immediate corollary of Theorem
8
.

Lemma 33 (Single-Step Augmented Sound Participants).

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
and
\mathchoice{\left\langle{C},{\Omega}\right\rangle}{\langle{C},{\Omega}\rangle}{\langle{C},{\Omega}\rangle}{\langle{C},{\Omega}\rangle}\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}\mathchoice{\left\langle{C^{\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime}}\right\rangle}{\langle{C^{\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime}}\rangle}{\langle{C^{\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime}}\rangle}{\langle{C^{\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime}}\rangle}
, then
\operatorname{rloc}({R})\subseteq\rho
.

Proof.

By induction on the step
\langle C,\Theta\rangle\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}\langle C^{\prime},\Theta^{\prime}\rangle
.

Corollary 5 (Augmented Sound Participants).

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
and
\mathchoice{\left\langle{C_{1}},{\Omega_{1}}\right\rangle}{\langle{C_{1}},{\Omega_{1}}\rangle}{\langle{C_{1}},{\Omega_{1}}\rangle}{\langle{C_{1}},{\Omega_{1}}\rangle}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{c}}\mathchoice{\left\langle{C_{2}},{\Omega_{2}}\right\rangle}{\langle{C_{2}},{\Omega_{2}}\rangle}{\langle{C_{2}},{\Omega_{2}}\rangle}{\langle{C_{2}},{\Omega_{2}}\rangle}\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}\mathchoice{\left\langle{C_{3}},{\Omega_{3}}\right\rangle}{\langle{C_{3}},{\Omega_{3}}\rangle}{\langle{C_{3}},{\Omega_{3}}\rangle}{\langle{C_{3}},{\Omega_{3}}\rangle}
,
then
\operatorname{rloc}({R})\setminus\top\subseteq(\Omega_{2}\setminus\Omega_{1})\cup(\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\cap\Omega_{2})
.

Proof.

Follows directly from Lemma
33
and Theorem
3
.
