Lemma 34.

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
then
\operatorname{SL}({C})=\varnothing
.

Proof.

Straightforward by induction on the typing derivation, noting that
\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill-after}}}
is not well-typed.
∎

Lemma 35.

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
then
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation,
noting that Lemma
34
guarantees that
\operatorname{SL}({C^{\prime}})=\varnothing
for all subterms
C^{\prime}
of
C
,
and that the augmented typing judgment only adds additional premises involving
\operatorname{SL}({C^{\prime}})

and changes each occurrence of
\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}
to
\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}
.
∎

Lemma 36.

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
and
\operatorname{SL}({C})=\varnothing
, then
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation, noting that
\operatorname{SL}({C})=\varnothing

guarantees that
\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill-after}}}
does not appear in any subterm of
C
.
∎

See
4

Proof.

Follows directly by Lemmas
35
and
36
.
∎

Theorem 9 (Surface Language Type Soundness).

If
\,\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
and
\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega
,
then whenever
\mathchoice{\left\langle{C},{\Omega}\right\rangle}{\langle{C},{\Omega}\rangle}{\langle{C},{\Omega}\rangle}{\langle{C},{\Omega}\rangle}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{c}}\mathchoice{\left\langle{C^{\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime}}\right\rangle}{\langle{C^{\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime}}\rangle}{\langle{C^{\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime}}\rangle}{\langle{C^{\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime}}\rangle}
,
either
C^{\prime}
is a value, or

\mathchoice{\left\langle{C^{\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime}}\right\rangle}{\langle{C^{\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime}}\rangle}{\langle{C^{\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime}}\rangle}{\langle{C^{\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime}}\rangle}
can step.

Proof.

A direct consequence of Theorems
2
,
3
and
4
.
∎

E.3.
Simulation Relation

Lemma 37 (Less-Than is Reflexive).

E\preceq E
for all network programs
E
.

Lemma 38 (Less-Than is Transitive).

If
E_{1}\preceq E_{2}
and
E_{2}\preceq E_{3}
then
E_{1}\preceq E_{3}
.

Lemma 39 (Less-Than Relation Preserves Free Variables).

If
E_{1}\preceq E_{2}
then
\operatorname{fv}({E_{1}})\subseteq\operatorname{fv}({E_{2}})
.

Lemma 40 (Merging Produces an Upper-Bound).

If
E_{1}\sqcup E_{2}=E
, then
E_{1}\preceq E
and
E_{2}\preceq E
.

Lemma 41 (Location Substitutions Preserve Less-Than).

For any location substitution
\sigma
, if
E_{1}\preceq E_{2}
then
E_{1}[\sigma]\preceq E_{2}[\sigma]
.

Lemma 42 (Type Substitutions Preserve Less-Than).

For any type substitution
\sigma
, if
E_{1}\preceq E_{2}
then
E_{1}[\sigma]\preceq E_{2}[\sigma]
.

Lemma 43 (Local Substitutions Preserve Less-Than).

For any local substitution
\sigma
, if
E_{1}\preceq E_{2}
then
E_{1}[\sigma]\preceq E_{2}[\sigma]
.

Lemma 44 (Substitutions Preserve Less-Than).

For any pair
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}
of variable substitutions such that

\sigma_{1}(X)\preceq\sigma_{2}(X)
for all
X
,
if
E_{1}\preceq E_{2}
, then
E_{1}[\sigma]\preceq E_{2}[\sigma]
.

Corollary 6.

If
E_{1}\preceq E_{2}
and
V_{1}\preceq V_{2}
, then
\mathchoice{E_{1}{\left[X\mapsto V_{1}\right]}}{E_{1}[X\mapsto V_{1}]}{E_{1}[X\mapsto V_{1}]}{E_{1}[X\mapsto V_{1}]}\preceq\mathchoice{E_{2}{\left[X\mapsto V_{2}\right]}}{E_{2}[X\mapsto V_{2}]}{E_{2}[X\mapsto V_{2}]}{E_{2}[X\mapsto V_{2}]}
.

Definition 7 (Network Program Collapsing).

Let
\operatorname{collapse}({E})
be the structurally homomorphic function on network programs such that
\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2}})=\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1}})\mathrel{\fatsemi}\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2}})
.
For instance,
\operatorname{collapse}({\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}let}}~{x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{E_{1}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}in}}~{E_{2}}})=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}let}}~{x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1}})}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}in}}~{\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2}})}
.

Lemma 45 (Collapsing Function is Less-Than).

\operatorname{collapse}({E})\preceq E
.

Proof.

By induction on
E
.
The only interesting case is when
E=E_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2}
, which holds by induction and as
\mathrel{\fatsemi}
preserves
\preceq
.
∎

Lemma 46 (Program Merging on Values).

If
E_{1}\sqcup E_{2}=E
, then
E_{1}
is a value
\Leftrightarrow

E_{2}
is a value
\Leftrightarrow

E
is a value.

Proof.

By induction on
E_{1}
, and analyzing the possible cases of
E_{2}
.
∎

Lemma 47 (Collapsing Preserves Program Merging).

If
E_{1}\sqcup E_{2}=E
then
\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1}})\sqcup\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2}})=\operatorname{collapse}({E})
.

Proof.

By induction on the definition of the merge function.
The only interesting case is when
E_{1}=E_{1,1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{1,2}
,
E_{2}=E_{2,1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2,2}
, and

E=(E_{1,1}\sqcup E_{2,1})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}(E_{1,2}\sqcup E_{2,2})
.
First suppose that
\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,1}})
is a value, in which case by Lemma
46
and the inductive
hypothesis
\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2,1}})
and
\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,1}})\sqcup\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2,1}})
are also values.
This implies that

\displaystyle\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1}})\sqcup\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2}})

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,1}})\mathrel{\fatsemi}\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,2}}))\sqcup(\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2,1}})\mathrel{\fatsemi}\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2,2}}))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,2}})\sqcup\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2,2}})

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,1}})\sqcup\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2,1}}))\mathrel{\fatsemi}(\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,2}})\sqcup\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2,2}}))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,1}\sqcup E_{2,1}})\mathrel{\fatsemi}\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,2}\sqcup E_{2,2}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({(E_{1,1}\sqcup E_{2,1})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}(E_{1,2}\sqcup E_{2,2})})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({E}).

Now if
\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,1}})
is not a value, we similarly have that

\displaystyle\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1}})\sqcup\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2}})

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,1}})\mathrel{\fatsemi}\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,2}}))\sqcup(\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2,1}})\mathrel{\fatsemi}\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2,2}}))

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,1}})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,2}}))\sqcup(\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2,1}})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2,2}}))

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,1}})\sqcup\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2,1}}))\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}(\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,2}})\sqcup\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2,2}}))

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,1}})\sqcup\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2,1}}))\mathrel{\fatsemi}(\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,2}})\sqcup\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2,2}}))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,1}\sqcup E_{2,1}})\mathrel{\fatsemi}\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1,2}\sqcup E_{2,2}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({(E_{1,1}\sqcup E_{2,1})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}(E_{1,2}\sqcup E_{2,2})})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({E}).

∎

Lemma 48 (Less-Than Relation Reflects Network-Program Merging).

If
E_{1}^{\prime}\preceq E_{1}
,
E_{2}^{\prime}\preceq E_{2}
,
\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1}^{\prime}})=E_{1}^{\prime}
,
\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2}^{\prime}})=E_{2}^{\prime}
, and
E_{1}\sqcup E_{2}=E
,
then there is some
E^{\prime}\preceq E
such that
E_{1}^{\prime}\sqcup E_{2}^{\prime}=E^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction and case analysis of
\preceq
.
The only interesting scenario is when the network programs are
\operatorname{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow-choice}}}
expressions or sequencing operations.

First consider the case when

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{\ell}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}\\
\mid{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}L}}}\Rightarrow{E_{1}^{\prime}}\end{array}\preceq\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{\ell}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}L}}\Rightarrow{E_{1}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}R}}\Rightarrow{E_{3}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{\ell}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}\\
\mid{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}R}}}\Rightarrow{E_{2}^{\prime}}\end{array}\preceq\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{\ell}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}L}}\Rightarrow{E_{4}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}R}}\Rightarrow{E_{2}}\end{array}

and

E=\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{\ell}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}L}}\Rightarrow{E_{1}\sqcup E_{4}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}R}}\Rightarrow{E_{3}\sqcup E_{2}}\end{array}

Then we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{\ell}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}\\
\mid{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}L}}}\Rightarrow{E_{1}^{\prime}}\end{array}\sqcup\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{\ell}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}\\
\mid{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}R}}}\Rightarrow{E_{2}^{\prime}}\end{array}=\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{\ell}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}L}}\Rightarrow{E_{1}^{\prime}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}R}}\Rightarrow{E_{2}^{\prime}}\end{array}

This suffices because by Lemma
40
,
E_{1}^{\prime}\preceq E_{1}\preceq E_{1}\sqcup E_{4}
, and
E_{2}^{\prime}\preceq E_{2}\preceq E_{3}\sqcup E_{2}
.
Now consider the case when

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{\ell}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}L}}\Rightarrow{E_{1,1}^{\prime}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}R}}\Rightarrow{E_{1,2}^{\prime}}\end{array}\preceq\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{\ell}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}L}}\Rightarrow{E_{1,1}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}R}}\Rightarrow{E_{1,2}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{\ell}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}L}}\Rightarrow{E_{2,1}^{\prime}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}R}}\Rightarrow{E_{2,2}^{\prime}}\end{array}\preceq\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{\ell}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}L}}\Rightarrow{E_{2,1}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}R}}\Rightarrow{E_{2,2}}\end{array}

and

E=\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{\ell}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}L}}\Rightarrow{E_{1,1}\sqcup E_{2,1}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}R}}\Rightarrow{E_{1,2}\sqcup E_{2,2}}\end{array}

Then by induction,
there is some
E_{3}\preceq E_{1,1}\sqcup E_{2,1}
where
E_{1,1}^{\prime}\sqcup E_{1,2}^{\prime}=E_{3}
,
and some
E_{4}\preceq E_{1,2}\sqcup E_{2,2}
where
E_{2,1}^{\prime}\sqcup E_{2,2}^{\prime}=E_{4}
.
Then the term

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{\ell}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}L}}\Rightarrow{E_{3}}\\
\mid\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.7344,0.4766,0}R}}\Rightarrow{E_{4}}\end{array}\preceq E

suffices.
The other
\operatorname{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow-choice}}}
cases are analogous to these two.

For sequencing operations, we note that the rule
E_{1}\preceq V\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2}
does not apply because

\operatorname{collapse}({V\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2}})=E_{2}\neq V\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2}
, which violates the assumptions.
Therefore the only possible scenario is
E_{1,1}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{1,2}^{\prime}\preceq E_{1,1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{1,2}
and

E_{2,1}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2,2}^{\prime}\preceq E_{2,1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2,2}
,
where
E=(E_{1,1}\sqcup E_{2,1})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}(E_{1,2}\sqcup E_{2,2})
.
Then by induction,
there is some
E_{3}\preceq E_{1,1}\sqcup E_{2,1}
where
E_{1,1}^{\prime}\sqcup E_{1,2}^{\prime}=E_{3}
,
and some
E_{4}\preceq E_{1,2}\sqcup E_{2,2}
where
E_{2,1}^{\prime}\sqcup E_{2,2}^{\prime}=E_{4}
,
so the term
E_{3}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{4}
suffices.
∎

Lemma 49 (Less-Than Reflects Values).

If
E_{1}\preceq E_{2}
and
\operatorname{Val}({E_{2}})
, then
\operatorname{Val}({E_{1}})
.

Proof.

By induction on the relation
E_{1}\preceq E_{2}
.
Note that the case when
E_{1}\preceq V\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2}
is impossible, because the right-hand side is not a value.
The other cases are straightforward, as all other rules (except
\operatorname{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow-choice}}}
expressions, which are also not values) are homomorphic.
∎

Lemma 50 (Merging Preserves Steps).

If
E_{1}\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}E_{1}^{\prime}
,
E_{2}\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}E_{2}^{\prime}
, and
E_{1}\sqcup E_{2}=E
, then there is some
E^{\prime}
and
E^{\prime\prime}
such that

E^{\prime}\preceq E^{\prime\prime}
,
E\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}E^{\prime\prime}
, and
E_{1}^{\prime}\sqcup E_{2}^{\prime}=E^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The interesting scenarios are for sequencing expressions and
\operatorname{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow-choice}}}
expressions.
Indeed, when
E_{1,1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2,1}\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}E_{1,1}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2,1}
and
E_{2,1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2,2}\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}E_{2,1}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2,2}
,
we can directly apply induction.
Otherwise if
E_{2,1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2,2}\mathrel{{\overset{\iota\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}E_{2,2}
because
\operatorname{Val}({E_{2,1}})
, then by Lemma
46

E_{1,1}
is a value, and hence this is the only step the left-hand side can take, so the result is immediate.
The same is symmetrically true if
E_{1,1}
is a value.

For
\operatorname{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow-choice}}}
expressions, note that the label
l
of each step is identical.
This means that both
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
receive the same direction
d
, and because they are required
to have that case defined, must both have at least this case defined.
∎

Lemma 51 (Simulating Less-Than is a Subrelation of Less-Than).

If
E_{1}\precsim E_{2}
then
E_{1}\preceq E_{2}
.

Lemma 52 (Simulating Less-Than is Reflexive).

E\precsim E
for all network programs
E
.

Lemma 53 (Simulating Less-Than is Transitive).

If
E_{1}\precsim E_{2}
and
E_{2}\precsim E_{3}
then
E_{1}\precsim E_{3}
.

Lemma 54 (Location Substitutions Preserve Simulating Less-Than).

For any location substitution
\sigma
, if
E_{1}\precsim E_{2}
then
E_{1}[\sigma]\precsim E_{2}[\sigma]
.

Lemma 55 (Type Substitutions Preserve Simulating Less-Than).

For any type substitution
\sigma
, if
E_{1}\precsim E_{2}
then
E_{1}[\sigma]\precsim E_{2}[\sigma]
.

Lemma 56 (Local Substitutions Preserve Simulating Less-Than).

For any local substitution
\sigma
, if
E_{1}\precsim E_{2}
then
E_{1}[\sigma]\precsim E_{2}[\sigma]
.

Lemma 57 (Substitutions Preserve Simulating Less-Than).

For any pair
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}
of variable substitutions such that

\sigma_{1}(X)\preceq\sigma_{2}(X)
for all
X
,
if
E_{1}\precsim E_{2}
, then
E_{1}[\sigma]\preceq E_{2}[\sigma]
.

Corollary 7.

If
E_{1}\precsim E_{2}
and
V_{1}\preceq V_{2}
, then
\mathchoice{E_{1}{\left[X\mapsto V_{1}\right]}}{E_{1}[X\mapsto V_{1}]}{E_{1}[X\mapsto V_{1}]}{E_{1}[X\mapsto V_{1}]}\preceq\mathchoice{E_{2}{\left[X\mapsto V_{2}\right]}}{E_{2}[X\mapsto V_{2}]}{E_{2}[X\mapsto V_{2}]}{E_{2}[X\mapsto V_{2}]}
.

Lemma 58 (Simulating Less-Than Preserves and Reflects Values).

If
E_{1}\precsim E_{2}
, then
\operatorname{Val}({E_{1}})\Leftrightarrow\operatorname{Val}({E_{2}})
.

Lemma 59 (Simulating Less-Than is Reachable from Less-Than).

If
E_{1}\preceq E_{2}
then there is some
E_{2}^{\prime}
such that

E_{1}\precsim E_{2}^{\prime}
and
L\triangleright E_{2}\mathrel{{\overset{\iota\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}^{*}}E_{2}^{\prime}
for any location
L
.
That is,
E_{2}
can reach
E_{2}^{\prime}
through a series of internal steps.

Proof.

By induction on the definition of
\preceq
.
For the case when
E_{1}\preceq V\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2}
,
we can first step
V\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2}\mathrel{{\overset{\iota\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}E_{2}
, and then
by induction we can step
E_{2}\mathrel{{\overset{\iota\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}^{*}}E_{2}^{\prime}
, where
E_{1}\precsim E_{2}^{\prime}
, which is satisfactory.
For those cases with a single head in the expression, apply the inductive hypothesis to the head of the expression, which is satisfactory.
Lastly, for those cases with multiple evaluation positions (function applications and pairs),
first apply the inductive hypothesis to the left expression.
If it yields a non-value expression, this should suffice.
Otherwise if it yields a value, also apply the inductive hypothesis to the right expression.
∎

Lemma 60 (Lifting Property).

If
E_{1}\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}E_{1}^{\prime}
and
E_{1}\precsim E_{2}
, then there is some
E_{2}^{\prime}
such that

E_{1}^{\prime}\preceq E_{2}^{\prime}
, and
E_{2}\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}E_{2}^{\prime}
.
That is, the following diagram holds:
E_{2}
E_{1}
E_{2}^{\prime}
E_{1}^{\prime}
R
R
\mathbin{\rotatebox[origin]{270.0}{$\succsim$}}
\mathbin{\rotatebox[origin]{270.0}{$\succeq$}}

Proof.

By induction on the definition of
\precsim
.
Each case follows by either applying the inductive hypothesis, or by recalling that substitution (of each sort) preserves the relation,
or produces terms which are related by
\preceq
.
∎

E.4.
Endpoint Projection

The following lemmas relate EPP to the substitution operations and the type system.
Notably, we show that EPP is preserved under each of the sorts of variable substitution,
with some specific conditions on the substitution depending on the sort.

Lemma 61 (Values Project to Values).

If
\operatorname{Val}({V})
then
\operatorname{Val}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket V\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}})
for any
L
.

Proof.

By induction on
V
.
∎

Lemma 62 (EPP Reduces Free Variables).

\operatorname{fv}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}})\subseteq\operatorname{fv}({C})
.

Proof.

By induction on
C
.
∎

Lemma 63 (Location Substitution Preserves EPP).

If
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}=E
and
L\notin\sigma
, then
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C[\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}=E[\sigma]
.

Proof.

By induction on
C
.
Each case follows directly by induction, noting that Lemmas
11
and
12

guarantee that the same sub-case of EPP will be selected by
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}
and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C[\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}
.
∎

Lemma 64.

If
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\alpha}}=E
and
\operatorname{NL}({C})\notin\sigma
then
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C[\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{\sigma(\alpha)}}{\llbracket C[\sigma]\rrbracket_{\sigma(\alpha)}}{\llbracket C[\sigma]\rrbracket_{\sigma(\alpha)}}{\llbracket C[\sigma]\rrbracket_{\sigma(\alpha)}}=E[\sigma]
.

Proof.

Similar to Lemma
63
.
By induction on
C
, noting that the same sub-case of EPP will be selected by
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\alpha}}
and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C[\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{\sigma(\alpha)}}{\llbracket C[\sigma]\rrbracket_{\sigma(\alpha)}}{\llbracket C[\sigma]\rrbracket_{\sigma(\alpha)}}{\llbracket C[\sigma]\rrbracket_{\sigma(\alpha)}}

because like location constants, variables are equal only to themselves,
and because any value
\alpha
may resolve to does not appear in
C
by assumption,
and so may only appear in
C[\sigma]
in places where
\alpha
appears in
C
.
For example, in the case of
C=\rho.e
, if
\alpha\in\rho
, and hence
\alpha\in\operatorname{fv}({\rho})
,
we have that
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\rho.e\right\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket\rho.e\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket\rho.e\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket\rho.e\rrbracket_{\alpha}}[\sigma]=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e})[\sigma]=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e[\sigma]})
.
Then because
\sigma(\alpha)\in\rho[\sigma]
, we have that

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\rho[\sigma].e[\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{\sigma(\alpha)}}{\llbracket\rho[\sigma].e[\sigma]\rrbracket_{\sigma(\alpha)}}{\llbracket\rho[\sigma].e[\sigma]\rrbracket_{\sigma(\alpha)}}{\llbracket\rho[\sigma].e[\sigma]\rrbracket_{\sigma(\alpha)}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e[\sigma]})
as expected.
Otherwise if
\alpha\notin\rho
, and hence
\alpha\notin\operatorname{fv}({\rho})
,
we have that
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\rho.e\right\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket\rho.e\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket\rho.e\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket\rho.e\rrbracket_{\alpha}}[\sigma]=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}[\sigma]=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}
.
Then because both
\alpha\notin\operatorname{fv}({\rho})
and
\sigma(\alpha)\notin\operatorname{NL}({\rho.e})=\operatorname{NL}({\rho})
,
we have that
\sigma(\alpha)\notin\rho[\sigma]
, and so

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\rho[\sigma].e[\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{\sigma(\alpha)}}{\llbracket\rho[\sigma].e[\sigma]\rrbracket_{\sigma(\alpha)}}{\llbracket\rho[\sigma].e[\sigma]\rrbracket_{\sigma(\alpha)}}{\llbracket\rho[\sigma].e[\sigma]\rrbracket_{\sigma(\alpha)}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}
as expected.
∎

Corollary 8.

If
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\alpha}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\alpha}}=E
and
L\notin\operatorname{NL}({C})
then
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\mathchoice{C{\left[\alpha\mapsto L\right]}}{C[\alpha\mapsto L]}{C[\alpha\mapsto L]}{C[\alpha\mapsto L]}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathchoice{C{\left[\alpha\mapsto L\right]}}{C[\alpha\mapsto L]}{C[\alpha\mapsto L]}{C[\alpha\mapsto L]}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathchoice{C{\left[\alpha\mapsto L\right]}}{C[\alpha\mapsto L]}{C[\alpha\mapsto L]}{C[\alpha\mapsto L]}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathchoice{C{\left[\alpha\mapsto L\right]}}{C[\alpha\mapsto L]}{C[\alpha\mapsto L]}{C[\alpha\mapsto L]}\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathchoice{E{\left[\alpha\mapsto L\right]}}{E[\alpha\mapsto L]}{E[\alpha\mapsto L]}{E[\alpha\mapsto L]}
.

Lemma 65 (Type Substitution Preserves EPP).

If
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}=E
, then for any type variable substitution
\sigma
we have that
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C[\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}=E[\sigma]
.

Proof.

By induction on
C
.
All cases follow directly by induction,
noting that no location or location set in
C
will be affected by the substitution,
so the same sub-case of EPP is selected.
∎

Lemma 66 (EPP is Fully Collapsed).

If
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}=E
then
\operatorname{collapse}({E})=E
.

Proof.

By induction on
C
.
Note that in the definition of EPP, the collapsing sequencing function
\mathrel{\fatsemi}
is always
used instead of the primitive
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}
for sequencing two programs.
Therefore each case follows directly by induction, and specifically because of the logic that
if
\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1}})=E_{1}
and
\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2}})=E_{2}
, then

\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1}\mathrel{\fatsemi}E_{2}})=\operatorname{collapse}({E_{1}})\mathrel{\fatsemi}\operatorname{collapse}({E_{2}})=E_{1}\mathrel{\fatsemi}E_{2}
.
∎

Lemma 67 (Member Local Substitution Preserves EPP).

If
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}=E
and
L\in\rho
, then for any local variable substitution
\sigma
we have that
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C[\rho|\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}=\operatorname{collapse}({E[\sigma]})
.

Proof.

By induction on
C
, noting that no location or location sets in
C
will be affected by the substitution.
The interesting cases are for
\rho^{\prime}.e
and when
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}
can appear in the projection of
C
, such as
C=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho^{\prime}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}}
.

•

If
\rho.e
and
L\in\rho^{\prime}
, we have that

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket(\rho^{\prime}.e)[\rho|\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket(\rho^{\prime}.e)[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket(\rho^{\prime}.e)[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket(\rho^{\prime}.e)[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\rho^{\prime}.e[\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\rho^{\prime}.e[\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\rho^{\prime}.e[\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\rho^{\prime}.e[\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e[\sigma]})=\operatorname{collapse}({\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e[\sigma]})}).

Otherwise if
L\notin\rho^{\prime}
then
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\rho^{\prime}.e[\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\rho^{\prime}.e[\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\rho^{\prime}.e[\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\rho^{\prime}.e[\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}=\operatorname{collapse}({\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}})
.

•

For
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho^{\prime}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}}
and
L\in\rho^{\prime}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\llbracket(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho^{\prime}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}})[\rho|\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho^{\prime}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}})[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho^{\prime}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}})[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho^{\prime}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}})[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho^{\prime}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho^{\prime}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho^{\prime}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho^{\prime}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]}\rrbracket_{L}}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}let}}~{x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}in}}~{\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}let}}~{x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}in}}~{\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}let}}~{x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}[\sigma]}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}in}}~{\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}[\sigma]}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}let}}~{x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}in}}~{\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}})[\sigma]}).

Otherwise if
L\notin\rho^{\prime}
then using Lemma
66
we see that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho^{\prime}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho^{\prime}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho^{\prime}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho^{\prime}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]}\rrbracket_{L}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}[\sigma]})\mathrel{\fatsemi}\operatorname{collapse}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}[\sigma]})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({(\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}})[\sigma]}).

•

For
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho^{\prime}}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}~(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}})~(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}})
, if
L\in\rho^{\prime}
the argument is straightforward by induction.
Now consider the case when
L\notin\rho^{\prime}
.
We have that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho^{\prime}}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}}\end{array}\right)[\rho|\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho^{\prime}}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}}\end{array}\right)[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho^{\prime}}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}}\end{array}\right)[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho^{\prime}}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}}\end{array}\right)[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho^{\prime}}~{C[\rho|\sigma]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]}\end{array}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho^{\prime}}~{C[\rho|\sigma]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]}\end{array}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho^{\prime}}~{C[\rho|\sigma]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]}\end{array}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho^{\prime}}~{C[\rho|\sigma]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]}\end{array}\rrbracket_{L}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C[\rho|\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}\sqcup\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}[\sigma]})\mathrel{\fatsemi}\operatorname{collapse}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}[\sigma]})\sqcup\operatorname{collapse}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}[\sigma]})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({(\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}\sqcup\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}})[\sigma]}),

where the final equality uses Lemma
47
.

∎

Lemma 68 (Non-Member Local Substitution Preserves EPP).

If
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}=E
and
L\notin\rho
, then for any local variable substitution
\sigma
we have that
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C[\rho|\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}=E
.

Proof.

The proof is nearly identical to Lemma
67
.
∎

Corollary 9 (Local Substitution Preserves EPP).

If
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}=E
, then for any local variable substitution
\sigma
there is some
E^{\prime}\preceq E
such that
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C[\rho|\sigma]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\rho|\sigma]\rrbracket_{L}}=E^{\prime}
.

Proof.

If
L\in\rho
then by Lemmas
45
and
67
,
E^{\prime}=\operatorname{collapse}({E[\sigma]})
suffices.
Otherwise if
L\notin\rho
, then by Lemma
68
and reflexivity of
\preceq
,
E^{\prime}=E
suffices.
∎

Definition 8.

For a choreographic variable substitution
\sigma_{1}
and a network-program variable substitution
\sigma_{2}
,
say that
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\sigma_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}=\sigma_{2}
if and only if

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\sigma_{1}(X)\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\sigma_{1}(X)\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\sigma_{1}(X)\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\sigma_{1}(X)\rrbracket_{L}}=\sigma_{2}(X)
for all program variables
X
.

Lemma 69 (Substitution Preserves EPP).

If
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}=E
and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\sigma_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}=\sigma_{2}
, then
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C[\sigma_{1}]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}=\operatorname{collapse}({E[\sigma_{2}]})
.

Proof.

By induction on
C
.

•

If
C=X
, then
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket X[\sigma_{1}]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket X[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket X[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket X[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\sigma_{1}(X)\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\sigma_{1}(X)\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\sigma_{1}(X)\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\sigma_{1}(X)\rrbracket_{L}}=\sigma_{2}(X)
by the assumption
and by Lemma
66
.

•

Let
C=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}}
.
If
L\in\rho
, the conclusion follows immediately by induction.
Otherwise if
L\notin\rho
, then

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\llbracket(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}})[\sigma_{1}]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}})[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}})[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}})[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}[\sigma_{1}]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}[\sigma_{1}]}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}[\sigma_{1}]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}[\sigma_{1}]}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}[\sigma_{1}]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}[\sigma_{1}]}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}[\sigma_{1}]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}[\sigma_{1}]}\rrbracket_{L}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}[\sigma_{1}]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}[\sigma_{1}]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}[\sigma_{2}]})\mathrel{\fatsemi}\operatorname{collapse}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}[\sigma_{2}]})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({(\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}})[\sigma_{2}]})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}}\rrbracket_{L}}[\sigma_{2}]}).

•

Let
C=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}~(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}})~(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}})
.
If
L\in\rho
, the conclusion follows immediately by induction.
Otherwise if
L\notin\rho
then,
noting that
X\notin\operatorname{fv}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}})
and
Y\notin\operatorname{fv}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}})
,
we have that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}}\end{array}\right)[\sigma_{1}]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}}\end{array}\right)[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}}\end{array}\right)[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}}\end{array}\right)[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho}~{C[\sigma_{1}]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}[X\mapsto X,Y\mapsto\sigma_{1}(Y)]}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}[X\mapsto X,Y\mapsto\sigma_{1}(Y)]}\end{array}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho}~{C[\sigma_{1}]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}[X\mapsto X,Y\mapsto\sigma_{1}(Y)]}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}[X\mapsto X,Y\mapsto\sigma_{1}(Y)]}\end{array}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho}~{C[\sigma_{1}]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}[X\mapsto X,Y\mapsto\sigma_{1}(Y)]}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}[X\mapsto X,Y\mapsto\sigma_{1}(Y)]}\end{array}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho}~{C[\sigma_{1}]}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}[X\mapsto X,Y\mapsto\sigma_{1}(Y)]}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}[X\mapsto X,Y\mapsto\sigma_{1}(Y)]}\end{array}\rrbracket_{L}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C[\sigma_{1}]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[\sigma_{1}]\rrbracket_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}[X\mapsto X,Y\mapsto\sigma_{1}(Y)]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[X\mapsto X,Y\mapsto\sigma_{1}(Y)]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[X\mapsto X,Y\mapsto\sigma_{1}(Y)]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}[X\mapsto X,Y\mapsto\sigma_{1}(Y)]\rrbracket_{L}}\sqcup\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}[X\mapsto X,Y\mapsto\sigma_{1}(Y)]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[X\mapsto X,Y\mapsto\sigma_{1}(Y)]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[X\mapsto X,Y\mapsto\sigma_{1}(Y)]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}[X\mapsto X,Y\mapsto\sigma_{1}(Y)]\rrbracket_{L}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}[\sigma_{2}]})\mathrel{\fatsemi}\operatorname{collapse}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}[\sigma_{2}]})\sqcup\operatorname{collapse}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}[\sigma_{2}]})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{collapse}({(\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}\sqcup\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}})[\sigma_{2}]}).

by applying Lemma
47
.

•

The other cases follow similar logic to those above.

∎

Corollary 10.

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C[X\mapsto V]\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[X\mapsto V]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[X\mapsto V]\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C[X\mapsto V]\rrbracket_{L}}\preceq\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}[X\mapsto\mathchoice{\left\llbracket V\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}]
.

Lemma 70 (Projection of Non-Participants).

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,
L\notin\rho
, and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}=E
, then
\operatorname{Val}({E})
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
.
Most cases are straightforward or follow similar logic to a case shown below.

•

(
S-Var
(
??
))
We have
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket X\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket X\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket X\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket X\rrbracket_{L}}=X
, which is a value.

•

(
S-Done
(
??
))
If the MLV is a value, we have either
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\rho.v\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\rho.v\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\rho.v\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\rho.v\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})
or
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\rho.v\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\rho.v\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\rho.v\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\rho.v\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}
, which is a value in either case.
Otherwise if the MLV is not a value then
L\notin\rho
, so
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\rho.e\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\rho.e\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\rho.e\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\rho.e\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}
.

•

(
S-Fun
(
??
))
If
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fun}}}_{\rho}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fun}}}_{\rho}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fun}}}_{\rho}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fun}}}_{\rho}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C}\rrbracket_{L}}
is defined, then it is either
\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fun}}
or
\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}
, both of which are values.

•

(
S-App
(
??
))
Let
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\rho}\tau_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{1}
,
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{2}
, and
\rho^{\prime}=\operatorname{tloc}({\Theta};{\tau_{1}})\cup\operatorname{tloc}({\Theta};{\tau_{2}})\cup\rho
.
By assumption
L\notin\rho^{\prime}\cup\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2}
, so we can apply induction to
C_{1}
and

to see that

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho^{\prime}}}C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho^{\prime}}}C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho^{\prime}}}C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho^{\prime}}}C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}
as both
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}
and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}
are values.

∎

Lemma 71 (Projection of Non-Owners).

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,
L\notin\rho\cup\operatorname{tloc}({\Theta};{\tau})
, and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}=E
, then
E=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}
or
E=X
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
.
Most cases are straightforward or follow similar logic to a case shown below.

.

\rho^{\prime}=\operatorname{tloc}({\Theta};{\mathchoice{\tau{\left[\alpha\mapsto\ell\right]}}{\tau[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{\tau[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{\tau[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}})\cup\mathchoice{\rho{\left[\alpha\mapsto\ell\right]}}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{\rho[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}

By assumption,

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho^{\prime}}}\ell\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho^{\prime}}}\ell\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho^{\prime}}}\ell\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho^{\prime}}}\ell\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}
.

,
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}

Therefore the projection is

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho^{\prime}}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}~(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}})~(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}})\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho^{\prime}}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}~(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}})~(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}})\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho^{\prime}}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}~(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}})~(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}})\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho^{\prime}}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}~(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}})~(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}})\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}\sqcup\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}\sqcup\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}
.
By the assumption that the projection exists, it must be that
X\notin\operatorname{fv}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}})
,
Y\notin\operatorname{fv}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}})
, and the merge
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}\sqcup\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}
exists.
If either
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket_{L}}
or
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket_{L}}
equals
\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}
, they both must be, and hence their merge equals
\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}
.
If either is a variable, they both must be the same variable by the fact that the merge exists, and hence their merge is a variable.
The argument for the other elimination forms is similar.

∎

Lemma 72 (Projection of Non-Participant Values).

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}V\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,
\operatorname{Val}({V})
and
L\notin\operatorname{tloc}({\Theta};{\tau})
, then
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket V\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}V\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
, similarly to Lemma
70
.
Note, however, that this Lemma is different than Lemma
70

because that there we pre-suppose that
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}
exists, whereas here we do not.
∎

Lemma 73.

If
L\notin\operatorname{cloc}({C})
and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}=E
, then
\operatorname{Val}({E})
.

Proof.

By induction on
C
.
∎

Lemma 74.

If
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}=E
where
\operatorname{Val}({E})
, then
L\notin\operatorname{cloc}({C})
.

Proof.

By induction on
C
.
∎

E.5.
Completeness, Soundness, and Deadlock-Freedom

Lemma 75 (Non-Participant Local Completeness).

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,
\langle C,\Omega\rangle\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}\langle C^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime}\rangle
,

L\in\Omega\setminus\operatorname{rloc}({R})
,
and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}=E
,
then there is some
E^{\prime}\preceq E
such that
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}=E^{\prime}
.
That is, the following diagram holds.
C
C^{\prime}
E
E^{\prime}
L\notin R
c
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\cdot\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket_{L}}
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\cdot\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket_{L}}
\succeq

Proof.

By induction on the step
\langle C,\Omega\rangle\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}\langle C^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime}\rangle
.

\Theta,F\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mu\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\rho_{1}}\tau_{2},X\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mu\tau_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{1}

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}V\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{2}

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket f\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho}}V\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket f\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho}}V\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket f\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho}}V\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket f\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho}}V\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathchoice{\left\llbracket V\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}},

.

23
,
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}\mathchoice{C{\left[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0muX\mapsto V\right]}}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0muX\mapsto V]}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0muX\mapsto V]}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0muX\mapsto V]}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{1}

is either
a unit
\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}
or variable
Z

Z\preceq\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}
and
\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}\preceq\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}

))
We handle the case when the function’s type variable is a location.
The assumptions are that

\Theta,F\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mu\forall{\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu*_{\mathsf{loc}}}[{\rho_{1}}]\ldotp{\tau},\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu*_{\mathsf{loc}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{1}
,

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash}\ell\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}::}*_{\mathsf{loc}}
,
and
L\notin\rho=\operatorname{tloc}({\Theta};{\mathchoice{\tau{\left[\alpha\mapsto\ell\right]}}{\tau[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{\tau[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{\tau[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}})\cup\mathchoice{\rho_{1}{\left[\alpha\mapsto\ell\right]}}{\rho_{1}[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{\rho_{1}[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{\rho_{1}[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}
.
The left side of the step projects to
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket f\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho}}\ell\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket f\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho}}\ell\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket f\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho}}\ell\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket f\mathbin{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\$}}_{\rho}}\ell\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}
,
where
f=\operatorname{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}tfun}}}{F}({\alpha})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C}
.
By Lemma
23
,
\Theta,\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu*_{\mathsf{loc}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}\mathchoice{C{\left[F\mapsto f\right]}}{C[F\mapsto f]}{C[F\mapsto f]}{C[F\mapsto f]}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{1}
,
and by Lemma
17
,

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}\mathchoice{C{\left[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell\right]}}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell]}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\mathchoice{\tau{\left[\alpha\mapsto\ell\right]}}{\tau[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{\tau[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{\tau[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\mathchoice{\rho_{1}{\left[\alpha\mapsto\ell\right]}}{\rho_{1}[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{\rho_{1}[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{\rho_{1}[\alpha\mapsto\ell]}
.
Therefore as
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}
must exist by the definition of EPP on
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}tfun}}
, by Lemma
70

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\mathchoice{C{\left[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell\right]}}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell]}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathchoice{C{\left[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell\right]}}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell]}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathchoice{C{\left[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell\right]}}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell]}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\mathchoice{C{\left[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell\right]}}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell]}{C[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0mu\alpha\mapsto\ell]}\rrbracket_{L}}
is either a unit or variable.
The arguments when the function’s type variable is a location set, program type, or local type are analogous.

, and the right side projects to
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket(\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2}).v\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket(\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2}).v\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket(\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2}).v\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket(\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2}).v\rrbracket_{L}}

Because

Corollary 11.

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,
\langle C,\Omega\rangle\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}\langle C^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime}\rangle
,

L\in\Omega\setminus\operatorname{rloc}({R})
,
and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}=E
,
then there is some
E^{\prime}\preceq E
such that
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}=E^{\prime}
.

Proof.

If
L\notin\operatorname{SL}({C})
, then this follows immediately by Lemma
75
.
Otherwise if
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C})
, then it either follows by setting
E^{\prime}=E
in the
case that the reduction does not occur in the scope of the
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}
expression that
L

is executing, and otherwise if it does, the result also follows by applying Lemma
75

to that subexpression.
∎

Lemma 76 (Participant Local Completeness).

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,
\langle C,\Omega\rangle\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}\langle C^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime}\rangle
,

L\in\Omega\cup\operatorname{rloc}({R})
,

R
is not a
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}
step,
and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}=E
, there is some
E_{1}^{\prime}
and
E_{2}^{\prime}
such that
E_{1}^{\prime}\preceq E_{2}^{\prime}
,
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}=E_{1}^{\prime}
, and
L\triangleright E\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\mathchoice{\left\llbracket R\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}}}{}^{+}}E_{2}^{\prime}
.
That is, the following diagram holds.
C
C^{\prime}
E
E^{\prime}
\succeq
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}
L\in R
c
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\cdot\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket_{L}}

+

Proof.

By induction on the step
\langle C,\Omega\rangle\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}\langle C^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime}\rangle
.

\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}}\end{array}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
