\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}^{\prime}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}^{\prime}}\end{array}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}^{\prime}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}^{\prime}}\end{array}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}case}}_{\rho}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}^{\prime}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}^{\prime}}\end{array}\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}^{\prime}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}\sqcup\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}^{\prime}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}^{\prime}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}\sqcup\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}^{\prime}\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{L}}.

Using Lemma
50
allows the required steps to be made on the right-hand side.
The other out-of-order steps follow similar logic.

∎

Corollary 12.

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,
\langle C,\Omega\rangle\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}\langle C^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime}\rangle
,

L\in\Omega\cup\operatorname{rloc}({R})
,

R
is not a
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}
step,
and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}=E
, there is some
E_{1}^{\prime}
and
E_{2}^{\prime}
such that
E_{1}^{\prime}\preceq E_{2}^{\prime}
,
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}=E_{1}^{\prime}
, and
L\triangleright E\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\mathchoice{\left\llbracket R\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}}}{}^{+}}E_{2}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

If
L\notin\operatorname{SL}({C})
, then this follows immediately by Lemma
76
.
Otherwise if
L\in\operatorname{SL}({C})
, then the reduction must occur in the scope of the
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}
expression that
L
is executing,
and the result also follows by applying Lemma
76
to that subexpression.
∎

Lemma 77 (Kill-Step Local Completeness).

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,
\langle C,\Omega\rangle\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}({L})}}{}_{c}}\langle C^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime}\rangle
,

L\in\Omega
,
and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}=E
, then
E\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}exit}}}}{}}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}
.

Proof.

By induction on the step, similarly to Lemma
76
.
For the step
C-Kill
(
??
) where
\langle~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{V},\Omega\rangle\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}\langle V,\Omega\setminus\{L\}\rangle
,
the projection for
L
simply steps as
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket V\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}exit}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}exit}}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}exit}}}}{}}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}

because by Lemma
61
,
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket V\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket V\rrbracket_{L}}
is a value.
If instead the step
C-KillI
(
??
) occurs and
\langle\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}~{L}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}after}}~{C},\Omega\rangle\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}\langle C,\Omega\setminus\{L\}\rangle
,
the projection for
L
steps as
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}exit}}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}exit}}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}exit}}}}{}}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}

because by Lemma
73
,
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}
is a value.
∎

Definition 9 (System Label Extraction).

The label extraction function
\lfloor{l_{S}}\rfloor_{L}
is a partial function
which maps system labels to network program labels as follows:

\lfloor{\iota_{L_{1}}}\rfloor_{L}=\begin{cases}\iota&\text{if}~L=L_{1}\\
\text{undefined}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\lfloor{L_{1}.m\rightsquigarrow\rho_{2}}\rfloor_{L}=\begin{cases}{m}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rightsquigarrow}}{\rho_{2}}&\text{if}~L=L_{1}\\
{L_{1}}.{m}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rightsquigarrow}}&\text{if}~L\neq L_{1}~\text{and}~L\in\rho_{2}\\
\text{undefined}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\lfloor{{L_{1}}.\mathtt{fork}({L_{2}},{E})}\rfloor_{L}=\begin{cases}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fork}}({L_{2}},{E})&\text{if}~L=L_{1}\\
\text{undefined}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\lfloor{\mathtt{kill}({L_{1}})}\rfloor_{L}=\begin{cases}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}exit}}&\text{if}~L=L_{1}\\
\text{undefined}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Definition 10 (Label Compatibility).

Say that a system label
l
is compatible with a pair
(\Pi,\Pi^{\prime})
of systems if and only if

(1)

whenever
l
is not
\mathtt{kill}
, we have that for all
L\in\operatorname{loc}({l})
, both
L\in\operatorname{dom}({\Pi})

and
L\triangleright\Pi(L)\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\lfloor{l}\rfloor_{L}}}{}}\Pi^{\prime}(L)
, and

(2)

if
l=\mathtt{kill}({L})
, then
L\in\operatorname{dom}({\Pi})
,

L\triangleright\Pi(L)\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}exit}}}}{}}E^{\prime}
, and
L\notin\operatorname{dom}({\Pi^{\prime}})
, and

(2)

if
L\in\operatorname{dom}({\Pi})\setminus\operatorname{loc}({l})
, then

\Pi(L)=\Pi^{\prime}(L)
.

Lemma 78 (Single-Step Combining).

If
l
is compatible with
(\Pi,\Pi^{\prime})
, then
\Pi\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{S}}\Pi^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By case analysis of the label
l
, noting that
\lfloor{l}\rfloor_{L}
is defined precisely
when
L\in\operatorname{loc}({l})
.
∎

Definition 11 (catMaybes).

Let
\mathsf{catMaybes}:\mathsf{list}({\mathsf{maybe}({t})})\to\mathsf{list}({t})
be the (meta)function
which concatenates all defined entries in a list.
For instance,

\mathsf{catMaybes}([1~,~\text{undefined}~,~2~,~3~,~\text{undefined}])=[1~,~2~,~3].

Lemma 79 (System-Step Combining).

For a sequence
l_{1}~,~l_{2}~,~\ldots~,~l_{n}
of system labels which are not
\mathtt{fork}
or
\mathtt{kill}
, if

(1)

L\triangleright\Pi(L)\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\mathsf{catMaybes}([\lfloor{l_{1}}\rfloor_{L}~,~\lfloor{l_{2}}\rfloor_{L}~,~\ldots~,~\lfloor{l_{n}}\rfloor_{L}])}}{}^{*}}\Pi^{\prime}(L)

for all
L\in\operatorname{dom}({\Pi})
,

(2)

for all
i
, if
L\in\operatorname{loc}({l_{i}})
then
L\in\operatorname{dom}({\Pi})
,

then
\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{l_{1}~,~l_{2}~,~\ldots~,~l_{n}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on the length of the reduction, applying Lemma
78
repeatedly,
and noting that the domain of the system never changes as no locations are spawned or killed.
∎

Lemma 80 (Redex Projection is Equivalent to Extraction).

For all locations
L
and redices
R
,

\mathsf{catMaybes}([\lfloor{S}\rfloor_{L}\mid l\in\mathchoice{\left\llbracket R\right\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}])=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket R\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}.

That is, the subsequence of system labels in
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket R\right\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}
which involve a location
L

is given precisely by the single-location projection
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket R\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
R
.
∎

Lemma 81.

If
\langle C,\Omega\rangle\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{{L}.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fork}}({L^{\prime}},{C^{\prime\prime}})}}{}_{c}}\langle C^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime}\rangle
,

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,

\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega
, and

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\right\rrbracket_{L^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket_{L^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket_{L^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket_{L^{\prime}}}\preceq E^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L^{\prime}}}=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\right\rrbracket_{L^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket_{L^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket_{L^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket_{L^{\prime}}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}exit}}

noting that

, and hence

, so no other

the assumption that the left-hand side projects for

Lemma 82 (Single-Step Completeness).

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,

\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega
,

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}=\Pi
, and

\langle C,\Omega\rangle\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}\langle C^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime}\rangle
,
then there is some
\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
such that
\Pi_{1}^{\prime}\preceq\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
,
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}=\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
, and
\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\mathchoice{\left\llbracket R\right\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}}}{}^{+}_{S}}\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
.
That is, the following diagram holds.
C
C^{\prime}
\Pi
\Pi^{\prime}
\succeq
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}
R
c
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\cdot\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\cdot\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket R\right\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L}}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L}}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L}}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L}}}
+

Proof.

First the case for steps which are not
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fork}}
or
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}
.
By Lemma
79
and Lemma
80
,
it suffices to prove that

L\triangleright\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\mathchoice{\left\llbracket R\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}}}{}^{*}}\Pi_{2}^{\prime}(L)

and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}\preceq\Pi_{2}^{\prime}(L)
for each location
L\in\Omega
.
If
L\notin\operatorname{rloc}({R})
, this holds by Corollary
11
, noting that
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket R\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket R\rrbracket_{L}}
is empty.
Otherwise if
L\in\operatorname{rloc}({R})
, this is precisely Corollary
12
.
To apply Lemma
79
we also need to show that
\operatorname{rloc}({R})\subseteq\Omega
.
This follows by soundness of participants (Theorem
1
), and as
\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega
by assumption.
That is,
L\in\operatorname{rloc}({R})\subseteq\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega
.
As well, there is always at least one location in the system that makes a step.

For the case of a
{L}.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}fork}}({L^{\prime}},{C^{\prime\prime}})
step, for each location already in the system—in
\Omega
—we can
either apply Corollary
11
for
L^{\prime\prime}\neq L,L^{\prime}
, or Corollary
12
for
L
.
The new thread
L^{\prime}
does not need to make a step, but we must show that
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L^{\prime}}}\preceq\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\right\rrbracket_{L^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket_{L^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket_{L^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket_{L^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}exit}}
,
and that this projection exists—this is precisely Lemma
81
.

For a
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}kill}}({L})
step, for each location
L^{\prime}\neq L
, we apply Corollary
11
.
For
L
, since they were removed from
\Omega
and the system, we do not need to worry about their projection,
and can simply apply Lemma
77
to allow the system to perform a
\mathtt{kill}({L})
step.
∎

Lemma 83 (System Single-Step Lifting).

If
\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Pi_{1}\precsim\Pi_{2}
then there is some
\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
such that

\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{S}}\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
and
\Pi_{1}^{\prime}\preceq\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Follows via a case analysis of the step and using Lemma
60
, noting
that by definition if
\Pi_{1}\precsim\Pi_{2}
then
\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{1}})=\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{2}})
,
so for the
\mathtt{fork}
and
\mathtt{kill}
steps, the respective systems after the steps will
still have the same domains, and the network program of any spawned thread will be identical
in
\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
.
∎

Corollary 13.

If
\Pi_{1}\preceq\Pi_{2}
then there is some
\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
such that

\Pi_{1}\precsim\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
and
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Follows by Lemmas
59
and
78
, noting that
the reduction sequence taken by each location are all
\iota
steps, and hence can all happen independently.
∎

Lemma 84 (System Lifting Property).

If
\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{n}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Pi_{1}\preceq\Pi_{2}
then there is some
\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
and
k\geq n
such that

\Pi_{2}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{k}_{S}}\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
and
\Pi_{1}^{\prime}\preceq\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on the length
n
of the initial reduction sequence.
If
n=0
the conclusion is trivial by choosing
k=0
and
\Pi_{2}^{\prime}=\Pi_{2}
.
Otherwise suppose the reduction is
\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{n}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.
By induction, there is some
\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
and
k\geq n
where

\Pi_{2}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{k}_{S}}\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
and
\Pi_{1}^{\prime}\preceq\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
.
By Corollary
13
, we can step
\Pi_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{2}^{\prime\prime}
where
\Pi_{1}^{\prime}\precsim\Pi_{2}^{\prime\prime}
.
By Lemma
83
, we can take a step
\Pi_{2}^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow_{S}}\Pi_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}
to some
\Pi_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}
where
\Pi_{1}^{\prime\prime}\preceq\Pi_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
Then the reduction sequence
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{k}_{S}}\Pi_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{2}^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow_{S}}\Pi_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}
is precisely as is required.
∎

Theorem 10 (Completeness).

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,

\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega
,

\langle C,\Omega\rangle\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{n}_{c}}\langle C^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime}\rangle
,
and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}=\Pi
,
then there is some
k\geq n
,
\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
, and
\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
such that
\Pi_{1}^{\prime}\preceq\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
,
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}=\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
, and
\Pi\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{k}_{S}}\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on the number of steps
n
.
The case when
n=0
is trivial.
For
n>0
, we have a reduction sequence of the form

\langle C_{1},\Omega_{1}\rangle\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{n}_{c}}\langle C_{2},\Omega_{2}\rangle\mathrel{\Longrightarrow_{c}}\langle C_{3},\Omega_{3}\rangle.

By the inductive hypothesis, there is some
k\geq n
and
\Pi_{2}
where

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{2}}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{2}}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{2}}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{2}}}\preceq\Pi_{2}
and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{1}}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{1}}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{1}}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{1}}}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{k}_{S}}\Pi_{2}
.
By Type Preservation (Theorem
3
),
C_{2}
is typed as
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho_{2}
for some
\rho_{2}
,
and
\operatorname{NL}({\rho_{2}})\subseteq\Omega_{2}
.
Thus we can apply Lemma
82
to
C_{2}
to find some

\Pi_{3}
such that

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{3}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{3}}}{\llbracket C_{3}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{3}}}{\llbracket C_{3}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{3}}}{\llbracket C_{3}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{3}}}\preceq\Pi_{3}
and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{2}}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{2}}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{2}}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{2}}}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{+}_{S}}\Pi_{3}
.
By Lemma
84
, there is some
\Pi_{3}^{\prime}\succeq\Pi_{3}
such that

\Pi_{2}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{+}_{S}}\Pi_{3}^{\prime}
.
Then
\Pi_{3}^{\prime}
is satisfactory, as
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{1}}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{1}}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{1}}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{1}}}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{k}_{S}}\Pi_{2}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{+}_{S}}\Pi_{3}^{\prime}
and

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{3}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{3}}}{\llbracket C_{3}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{3}}}{\llbracket C_{3}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{3}}}{\llbracket C_{3}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{3}}}\preceq\Pi_{3}\preceq\Pi_{3}^{\prime}
.
The argument is summarized by the following diagram.
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{1}}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{1}}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{1}}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{1}}}
\Pi_{2}
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{2}}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{2}}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{2}}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{2}}}
\Pi_{3}^{\prime}
\Pi_{3}
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{3}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{3}}}{\llbracket C_{3}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{3}}}{\llbracket C_{3}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{3}}}{\llbracket C_{3}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega_{3}}}
k
+
+
\mathbin{\rotatebox[origin]{270.0}{$\succeq$}}
\mathbin{\rotatebox[origin]{270.0}{$\succeq$}}
\mathbin{\rotatebox[origin]{270.0}{$\succeq$}}

∎

See
5

Proof.

Follows directly from Theorem
10
and Lemma

.
∎

Lemma 85 (Single-Step Participant Domain).

If
\Pi\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{S}}\Pi^{\prime}
, then
\operatorname{loc}({l})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}({\Pi})
.

Proof.

By case analysis of the label
l
.
∎

Lemma 86 (Single-Step Non-Participant Invariance).

If
\Pi\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{S}}\Pi^{\prime}
and
L\in\operatorname{dom}({\Pi})\setminus\operatorname{loc}({l})
, then
\Pi^{\prime}(L)=\Pi(L)
.

Proof.

By case analysis of the label
l
.
∎

Lemma 87 (Non-Participant Invariance).

If
\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{l_{1},\ldots,l_{n}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi^{\prime}
,
L\in\operatorname{dom}({\Pi})
, and
L\notin\operatorname{loc}({l_{i}})
for each
i
, then
\Pi^{\prime}(L)=\Pi(L)
.

Proof.

By induction on
n
.
If
n=0
, the result is trivial.
Otherwise suppose that
\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{l_{1},\ldots,l_{n}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi^{\prime}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{l_{n+1}}}{}_{S}}\Pi^{\prime\prime}
,
and let
L\in\operatorname{dom}({\Pi})\setminus\bigcup_{i\leq n+1}\operatorname{loc}({l_{i}})
be arbitrary.
If
L\in\operatorname{dom}({\Pi^{\prime}})
, then the result holds by induction and Lemma
86
.
Otherwise, suppose that
L\notin\operatorname{dom}({\Pi^{\prime}})
.
Because
L\in\operatorname{dom}({\Pi})\setminus\operatorname{dom}({\Pi^{\prime}})
, it must be that
L
is a spawned thread
that was killed during the reduction sequence.
However, this is impossible, as then some label must be
l_{i}=\mathtt{kill}({L})
,
for which
L\in\operatorname{loc}({l_{i}})=\{L\}
, a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 88 (Single-Step Parallel Composition).

If
\Pi_{a}\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{S}}\Pi_{a}^{\prime}
and
\Pi_{b}
is such that
\Pi_{b}(L)=\Pi_{a}(L)
for all
L\in\operatorname{loc}({l})
,
then there is some
\Pi_{b}^{\prime}
where

(1)

\Pi_{b}\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{S}}\Pi_{b}^{\prime}
,

(2)

\Pi_{b}^{\prime}(L)=\Pi_{a}^{\prime}(L)
for all
L\in\operatorname{loc}({l})\cap\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{a}^{\prime}})
,

(3)

\Pi_{b}^{\prime}(L)=\Pi_{a}^{\prime}(L)
for all
L\in\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{a}^{\prime}})\setminus\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{a}})
, and

(4)

\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{b}})\setminus\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{b}^{\prime}})=\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{a}})\setminus\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{a}^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

By case analysis on
l
.
Note that if
l
is
\mathtt{fork}
we can, by identifying systems up-to choice of spawned location names,
ensure the spawned location is not in
\Pi_{b}
.
∎

Lemma 89 (Parallel Composition).

If
\Pi_{a}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{l_{1},\ldots,l_{n}}}{}_{S}}\Pi_{a}^{\prime}
and
\Pi_{b}
is such that
\Pi_{b}(L)=\Pi_{a}(L)

for all
i
and
L\in\operatorname{loc}({l_{i}})\cap\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{a}})
,
then there is some
\Pi_{b}^{\prime}
where

(1)

\Pi_{b}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{l_{1},\ldots,l_{n}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{b}^{\prime}
,

(2)

\Pi_{b}^{\prime}(L)=\Pi_{a}^{\prime}(L)
for all
i
and
L\in\operatorname{loc}({l_{i}})\cap\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{a}^{\prime}})
,

(3)

\Pi_{b}^{\prime}(L)=\Pi_{a}^{\prime}(L)
for all
L\in\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{a}^{\prime}})\setminus\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{a}})
, and

(4)

\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{b}})\setminus\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{b}^{\prime}})=\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{a}})\setminus\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{a}^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

By induction on
n
.
Without loss of generality, we assume the name of a spawned thread is not re-used once killed.
When
n=0
the result is trivial, so suppose that
\Pi_{a}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{l_{1},\ldots,l_{n}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{a}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{l_{n+1}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{a}^{\prime\prime}
.
By induction, there is
\Pi_{b}^{\prime}
satisfying (1)–(4).
To complete the proof, we apply Lemma
88
,
and hence must prove that
\Pi_{b}^{\prime}(L)=\Pi_{a}^{\prime}(L)
for all
L\in\operatorname{loc}({l_{n+1}})
.
By Lemma
85
,
\operatorname{loc}({l_{n+1}})\subseteq\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{a}^{\prime}})
, so
L\in\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{a}^{\prime}})
.
If
L\in\operatorname{loc}({l_{i}})
for any
i
, then the desired result holds by (2) of the inductive hypothesis,
so we instead assume that
L\notin\operatorname{loc}({l_{i}})
for each
i
.
Similarly if
L\notin\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{a}})
then the desired result holds by (3),
so we instead assume that
L\in\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{a}})
.
Then because
L\in\operatorname{loc}({l_{n+1}})\cap\operatorname{dom}({\Pi_{a}})
,
by assumption we have that
\Pi_{b}(L)=\Pi_{a}(b)
.
Finally, by Lemma
87
and (1) of the inductive hypothesis,
we have that both
\Pi_{a}(L)=\Pi_{a}^{\prime}(L)
and
\Pi_{b}(L)=\Pi_{b}^{\prime}(L)
,
so that
\Pi_{b}^{\prime}(L)=\Pi_{a}^{\prime}(L)
as desired.
∎

Lemma 90 (Parallel Confluence).

If
ls_{a}
and
ls_{b}
are two lists of system labels where

\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{a}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}
,

\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{b}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{2}
, and

\operatorname{loc}({ls_{a}})\cap\operatorname{loc}({ls_{b}})=\varnothing
,
then there is some
\Pi_{3}
where
\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{b}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{3}
and
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{a}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{3}
.

Proof.

Decompose
\Pi
into the disjoint union
\Pi=\Pi_{a}\uplus\Pi_{b}\uplus\Pi^{\prime}
of three sub-systems:

\Pi_{a}
with domain
\operatorname{loc}({ls_{a}})
,

\Pi_{b}
with domain
\operatorname{loc}({ls_{b}})
,
and
\Pi^{\prime}
with domain
\operatorname{dom}({\Pi})\setminus(\operatorname{loc}({ls_{a}})\cup\operatorname{loc}({ls_{b}}))
.
By Lemma
89
,
we can step
\Pi_{a}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{a}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{a}^{\prime}
and
\Pi_{b}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{b}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{b}^{\prime}
.
Without loss of generality, we let all newly-spawned locations in
\Pi_{a}^{\prime}
and
\Pi_{b}^{\prime}

have names that are disjoint and not in
\operatorname{dom}({\Pi})
.
Then by Lemmas
89
and
87
,
we claim that
\Pi_{3}=\Pi_{a}^{\prime}\uplus\Pi_{b}^{\prime}\uplus\Pi^{\prime}
suffices because

\Pi_{a}\uplus\Pi_{b}\uplus\Pi^{\prime}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{a}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{a}^{\prime}\uplus\Pi_{b}\uplus\Pi^{\prime}=\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{b}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{a}^{\prime}\uplus\Pi_{b}^{\prime}\uplus\Pi^{\prime}=\Pi_{3}

and
\Pi_{a}\uplus\Pi_{b}\uplus\Pi^{\prime}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{b}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{a}\uplus\Pi_{b}^{\prime}\uplus\Pi^{\prime}=\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{a}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{a}^{\prime}\uplus\Pi_{b}^{\prime}\uplus\Pi^{\prime}=\Pi_{3}
.
∎

Lemma 91 (Network Program Determinism).

If
L\triangleright E\mathrel{{\overset{l_{1}\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}E_{1}
and
L\triangleright E\mathrel{{\overset{l_{2}\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}E_{2}
,
then either (1)
l_{1}=l_{2}
, (2)
l_{1}={L^{\prime}}.{v_{1}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rightsquigarrow}}
and
l_{2}={L^{\prime}}.{v_{2}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rightsquigarrow}}
where
v_{1}\neq v_{2}
,
or (3)
l_{1}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e}\Rightarrow{e_{1}}})
and
l_{2}=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e}\Rightarrow{e_{2}}})
where
e_{1}\neq e_{2}
.

Proof.

By induction on the first step, and case analysis of the second step.
∎

Lemma 92 (System Per-Location Determinism).

If
\Pi\mathrel{{\overset{l_{1}\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{S}}\Pi_{1}
,
\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{2}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{2}
,
and
\operatorname{loc}({l_{1}})\cap\operatorname{loc}({ls_{2}})\neq\varnothing
, then there is
some
ls_{a},ls_{b}
, and
l_{1}^{\prime}
such that (1)
ls_{2}=ls_{a}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptl_{1}^{\prime}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{b}
,
(2)
\operatorname{loc}({l_{1}})\cap\operatorname{loc}({ls_{a}})=\varnothing
,
and (3) either
l_{1}^{\prime}=l_{1}
, or
l_{1}=L.\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e}\Rightarrow{e_{1}}})
and
l_{2}=L.\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e}\Rightarrow{e_{2}}})
where
e_{1}\neq e_{2}
.

Proof.

By induction on
ls_{2}
.
We cannot have that
ls_{2}=\epsilon
, for there should be some location in common to
ls_{2}
and
l_{1}
.
Otherwise suppose that
ls_{2}=l_{2}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{2}^{\prime}
.
First suppose that
\operatorname{loc}({l_{1}})\cap\operatorname{loc}({l_{2}})=\varnothing
.
Then it must be the case that
\operatorname{loc}({l_{1}})\cap\operatorname{loc}({ls_{2}^{\prime}})\neq\varnothing
,
so we may apply induction to
ls_{2}^{\prime}
to yield
ls_{a},ls_{b}
, and
l_{1}^{\prime}
.
Then
ls_{a}^{\prime}=l_{2}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{a}
,
ls_{b}^{\prime}=ls_{b}
, and
l_{1}^{\prime\prime}=l_{1}^{\prime}
suffice.
Otherwise suppose that there is some location
L\in\operatorname{loc}({l_{1}})\cap\operatorname{loc}({l_{2}})
.
Then by Lemma
91
,
\lfloor{l_{1}}\rfloor_{L}
and
\lfloor{l_{2}}\rfloor_{L}
are either equal,
receives of two different values, or local programs stepping to two different values.
We claim that
L
cannot receive two different values.
Indeed, the message sent by the sender
L^{\prime}\in\operatorname{loc}({l_{1}})\cap\operatorname{loc}({l_{2}})
is deterministic,
so the received values must be equal.
Thus choosing
ls_{a}^{\prime}=\epsilon
,
ls_{b}^{\prime}=ls_{2}^{\prime}
, and
l_{1}^{\prime}=l_{2}
suffices.
∎

Definition 12 (Prefix).

If
xs
and
ys
are two lists (containing any type of object), we define
xs\leq ys
to mean that
xs
is a prefix of
ys
.
That is,
xs\leq ys
if and only if there is some
zs
such that
ys=xs+zs
.

Definition 13 (Subsequence).

If
xs
and
ys
are two lists, we define
xs\subseteq ys
to mean that
xs
is a subsequence of
ys
.
That is,
xs\subseteq ys
if and only if there is some map
\sigma:\lvert{xs}\rvert\rightarrow\lvert{ys}\rvert

which is strictly order-preserving (i.e., if
i<j
then
\sigma(i)<\sigma(j)
)
and the values in the indices of
xs
correspond to the values in
ys
after
\sigma
.
That is, for all
i\in\lvert{xs}\rvert
,
xs[i]=ys[\sigma(i)]
.

Lemma 93 (System Step Mirroring).

If
\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}
and
\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{2}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{2}
,
where
ls_{2}
contains no steps of the form
L.\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e}\Rightarrow{e^{\prime}}})
,
then there is some
ls_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq ls_{2}
and
\Pi_{1}^{\prime}

where
\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
and
\forall L,\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L}\leq\lfloor{ls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}
.
That is, we can always “catch-up” each location from
\Pi_{1}
to their state in
\Pi_{2}
.

Proof.

By induction on
ls_{2}
.
If
ls_{2}=\epsilon
, then the conclusion is trivial.
Otherwise suppose that
ls_{2}=l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{2}^{\prime}
,
where
\Pi\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{S}}\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{2}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
.

•

First suppose that
\operatorname{loc}({l})\cap\operatorname{loc}({ls_{1}})\neq\varnothing
.
In this case, we apply Lemma
92
to find
ls_{a}
,
ls_{b}
where

ls_{1}=ls_{a}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptl+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{b}
and
\operatorname{loc}({l})\cap\operatorname{loc}({ls_{a}})=\varnothing
.
Note that because
l
cannot be a local program step, Lemma
92

guarantees the middle step label to be identical.
Say that
\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{a}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{a}\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{S}}\Pi_{b}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{b}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}
.
Then by applying Parallel Confluence (Lemma
90
),
we can step
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{a}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{b}
.
By induction on
ls_{2}^{\prime}
and
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{a}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{b}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{a}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}
,
we can find some
ls_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq ls_{a}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{b}
and
\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
where
\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime}

and
\forall L,\lfloor{ls_{2}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}\leq\lfloor{ls_{b}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}
.
Then
\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
and
ls_{1}^{\prime}
suffices because

–

ls_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq ls_{2}^{\prime}\subseteq l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{2}^{\prime}=ls_{2}
, and

–

for each
L
, noting that either
L\in\operatorname{loc}({l})
and
\lfloor{ls_{a}}\rfloor_{L}=\epsilon
,
or
L\notin\operatorname{loc}({l})
and
\lfloor{l}\rfloor_{L}=\epsilon
, we have that

\displaystyle\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{l}\rfloor_{L}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{2}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle\leq\lfloor{l}\rfloor_{L}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{a}}\rfloor_{L}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{b}}\rfloor_{L}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{ls_{a}}\rfloor_{L}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{l}\rfloor_{L}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{b}}\rfloor_{L}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{ls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}

The following diagram summarizes the argument,
where solid lines represent premises, and dashed lines represent conclusions.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{2}^{\prime}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{1}^{\prime}}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ls_{1}^{\prime}}

•

Finally, suppose that
\operatorname{loc}({l})\cap\operatorname{loc}({ls_{1}})=\varnothing
.
The idea is similar, but using Parallel Confluence on the entirety of
ls_{1}
to
step
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
.
The we apply induction on
ls_{2}^{\prime}
and
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime}

to find some
ls_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq ls_{1}
and
\Pi_{1}^{\prime\prime}
where
\Pi_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime\prime}

and
\forall L,\lfloor{ls_{2}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}\leq\lfloor{ls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}
.
Then
\Pi_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}
suffices because

–

ls_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq ls_{2}^{\prime}
, so
l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{2}^{\prime}=ls_{2}
, and

–

for each
L

\displaystyle\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{l}\rfloor_{L}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{2}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle\leq\lfloor{l}\rfloor_{L}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}

The following diagram summarizes this case:

Lemma 94 (Local Value Mirroring).

If
\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}
and there is some
v
,
e_{L}
, and
\eta_{L}
such that for all
L\in\rho
,
e_{L}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{*}}v
and
\Pi(L)=\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e_{L}})]
,
then there is some
\Pi_{1}^{\prime},ls_{1}^{\prime}
, and
ls_{2}
such that

(1)

ls_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq ls_{2}
,

(2)

\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{2}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\mathchoice{\Pi{\left[L\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]\right]}}{\Pi[L\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}{\Pi[L\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}{\Pi[L\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}
,

(3)

\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
, and

(4)

for all
L
,
\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L}\leq\lfloor{ls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}
.

Proof.

By induction on
ls_{1}
.
First if
ls_{1}=\epsilon
, then choosing
ls_{1}^{\prime}=ls_{2}=[L.\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e_{L}}\Rightarrow{v}})\mid L\in\rho]
for some arbitrary ordering of
\rho
suffices.
Otherwise suppose that
\Pi\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{S}}\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
.

•

First consider the case when
\rho\cap\operatorname{loc}({l})=\varnothing
.
Then
\Pi_{1}(L)=\Pi(L)
for all
L\in\rho
, so we can simply apply induction
to
\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
to arrive at the desired result.

•

Otherwise suppose that there is some
L\in\rho\cap\operatorname{loc}({l})
.
Then by Determinism (Lemma
91
), either
l=L.\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e_{L}}\Rightarrow{e_{L}^{\prime}}})
,
or
e_{L}
is a value and
l
is a different kind of step.

–

If
l=L.\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e_{L}}\Rightarrow{e_{L}^{\prime}}})
, then we can apply induction to
\Pi_{1}=\mathchoice{\Pi{\left[L\mapsto\eta_{L}[e_{L}^{\prime}]\right]}}{\Pi[L\mapsto\eta_{L}[e_{L}^{\prime}]]}{\Pi[L\mapsto\eta_{L}[e_{L}^{\prime}]]}{\Pi[L\mapsto\eta_{L}[e_{L}^{\prime}]]}

to produce
\Pi_{1}^{\prime\prime},ls_{1}^{\prime}
, and
ls_{2}
satisfying (1–4).
Then
\Pi_{1}^{\prime\prime},ls_{1}^{\prime}
, and
l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{2}
suffice because

(1)

ls_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq ls_{2}\Rightarrow ls_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{2}
,

(2)

\Pi\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{S}}\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{2}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\mathchoice{\Pi{\left[L\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]\right]}}{\Pi[L\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}{\Pi[L\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}{\Pi[L\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}
,

(3)

\Pi_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime\prime}
, and

(4)

for all
L^{\prime}\neq L
,

\displaystyle\lfloor{l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{2}}\rfloor_{L^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\lfloor{ls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}

and

\displaystyle\lfloor{l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{2}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e_{L}}\Rightarrow{v}})+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle\leq\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e_{L}}\Rightarrow{v}})+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}.

–

Now suppose that
e_{L}
is a value.
By Local Confluence, it must be the case that
e_{L}=v
.
We can apply induction to
\Pi_{1}
by reducing
\rho
to
\rho\setminus\{L\}
so that it satisfies the premises,
to produce some
\Pi_{1}^{\prime\prime},ls_{1}^{\prime}
, and
ls_{2}
that satisfy (1–4), for
ls_{1}
.
Then we claim that
\Pi_{1}^{\prime\prime},ls_{1}^{\prime}
and
ls_{s}
are satisfactory because

\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{2}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\mathchoice{\Pi{\left[L^{\prime}\in\rho\setminus\{L\}\mapsto\eta_{L^{\prime}}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]\right]}}{\Pi[L^{\prime}\in\rho\setminus\{L\}\mapsto\eta_{L^{\prime}}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}{\Pi[L^{\prime}\in\rho\setminus\{L\}\mapsto\eta_{L^{\prime}}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}{\Pi[L^{\prime}\in\rho\setminus\{L\}\mapsto\eta_{L^{\prime}}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}=\mathchoice{\Pi{\left[L^{\prime}\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L^{\prime}}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]\right]}}{\Pi[L^{\prime}\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L^{\prime}}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}{\Pi[L^{\prime}\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L^{\prime}}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}{\Pi[L^{\prime}\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L^{\prime}}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}
.

Lemma 95 (Local Program Mirroring).

If
\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}
and there is some
e
,
e_{L}
, and
\eta_{L}
such that for all
L\in\rho
,
e\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{*}}e_{L}
and
\Pi(L)=\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e_{L}})]
,
then there is some
e^{\prime},\Pi_{1}^{\prime},ls_{1}^{\prime}
, and
ls_{2}
such that

(1)

e\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{*}}e^{\prime}
,

(2)

ls_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq ls_{2}
,

(3)

\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{2}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\mathchoice{\Pi{\left[L\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e^{\prime}})]\right]}}{\Pi[L\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e^{\prime}})]]}{\Pi[L\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e^{\prime}})]]}{\Pi[L\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e^{\prime}})]]}
,

(4)

\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
, and

(5)

for all
L
,
\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L}\leq\lfloor{ls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}
.

Proof.

By induction on
ls_{1}
.
First if
ls_{1}=\epsilon
, then by repeated application of Local Confluence (Property
2
) there is
some
e^{\prime}
where
e_{L}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{*}}e^{\prime}
for all
L\in\rho
.
Then choosing
ls_{1}^{\prime}=ls_{2}=[L.\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e_{L}}\Rightarrow{e^{\prime}}})\mid L\in\rho]
for some arbitrary ordering of
\rho
suffices.
Otherwise suppose that
\Pi\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{S}}\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
.

•

First consider the case when
\rho\cap\operatorname{loc}({l})=\varnothing
.
Then
\Pi_{1}(L)=\Pi(L)
for all
L\in\rho
, so we can simply apply induction
to
\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
to arrive at the desired result.

•

Otherwise suppose that there is some
L\in\rho\cap\operatorname{loc}({l})
.
Then by Determinism (Lemma
91
), either
l=L.\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e_{L}}\Rightarrow{e_{L}^{\prime}}})
,
or
e_{L}
is a value and
l
is a different kind of step.

–

If
l=L.\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e_{L}}\Rightarrow{e_{L}^{\prime}}})
, then we can apply induction to
\Pi_{1}=\mathchoice{\Pi{\left[L\mapsto\eta_{L}[e_{L}^{\prime}]\right]}}{\Pi[L\mapsto\eta_{L}[e_{L}^{\prime}]]}{\Pi[L\mapsto\eta_{L}[e_{L}^{\prime}]]}{\Pi[L\mapsto\eta_{L}[e_{L}^{\prime}]]}

to produce some
e^{\prime},\Pi_{1}^{\prime\prime},ls_{1}^{\prime}
, and
ls_{2}
satisfying (1–5).
Then
e^{\prime},\Pi_{1}^{\prime\prime},ls_{1}^{\prime}
, and
l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{2}
suffice because

(1)

e\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{*}}e^{\prime}
,

(2)

ls_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq ls_{2}\Rightarrow ls_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{2}
,

(3)

\Pi\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{S}}\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{2}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\mathchoice{\Pi{\left[L\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e^{\prime}})]\right]}}{\Pi[L\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e^{\prime}})]]}{\Pi[L\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e^{\prime}})]]}{\Pi[L\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e^{\prime}})]]}
,

(4)

\Pi_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime\prime}
, and

(5)

for all
L^{\prime}\neq L
,

\displaystyle\lfloor{l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{2}}\rfloor_{L^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\lfloor{ls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}

and

\displaystyle\lfloor{l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{2}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e_{L}}\Rightarrow{e_{L}^{\prime}}})+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle\leq\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e_{L}}\Rightarrow{e_{L}^{\prime}}})+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}.

–

Now suppose that
e_{L}=v
is a value.
Then by Local Confluence,
e_{L^{\prime}}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{*}}v
for each
L^{\prime}\in\rho
.
Then we can apply Lemma
94
to
\Pi_{1}
by reducing
\rho
to
\rho\setminus\{L\}
so that it satisfies the premises,
to produce some
\Pi_{1}^{\prime\prime},ls_{1}^{\prime}
, and
ls_{2}
that satisfy (1–4) of Lemma
94
.
Then
v,\Pi_{1}^{\prime\prime},ls_{1}^{\prime}
and
ls_{2}
satisfy the conclusion because

(1)

e\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{*}}e_{L}\mathrel{\longrightarrow^{*}}v
,

(2)

ls_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq ls_{2}
,

(3)

\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{2}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\mathchoice{\Pi_{1}{\left[L^{\prime}\in\rho\setminus\{L\}\mapsto\eta_{L^{\prime}}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]\right]}}{\Pi_{1}[L^{\prime}\in\rho\setminus\{L\}\mapsto\eta_{L^{\prime}}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}{\Pi_{1}[L^{\prime}\in\rho\setminus\{L\}\mapsto\eta_{L^{\prime}}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}{\Pi_{1}[L^{\prime}\in\rho\setminus\{L\}\mapsto\eta_{L^{\prime}}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}
,
so
\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{2}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\mathchoice{\Pi{\left[L^{\prime}\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L^{\prime}}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]\right]}}{\Pi[L^{\prime}\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L^{\prime}}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}{\Pi[L^{\prime}\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L^{\prime}}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}{\Pi[L^{\prime}\in\rho\mapsto\eta_{L^{\prime}}[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})]]}
,

(4)

\Pi_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}^{\prime\prime}
, and

(5)

for
L^{\prime}\in\operatorname{loc}({l})
we have that
\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L^{\prime}}=\epsilon
because
either
L^{\prime}\in\rho
and
e_{L}=v
or
L^{\prime}\notin\rho
, so

\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L^{\prime}}=\epsilon\leq\lfloor{l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L^{\prime}}
,
and for
L^{\prime}\notin\operatorname{loc}({l})
we have that

\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L^{\prime}}\leq\lfloor{ls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L^{\prime}}=\lfloor{l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L^{\prime}}
.

∎

Lemma 96 (Projections of Choreographies with Local Steps).

If
C\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{{\rho}.({e}\rightarrow{e^{\prime}})}}{}_{c}}C^{\prime}
, then for every
L\in\rho
where
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}=E
,
there is some evaluation context
\eta
such that
E=\eta[\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e})]
.

Proof.

By induction on the step.
The case when
C=\rho.e
is clear.
For steps in a choreographic evaluation context, we can simply use induction and add to the network program context.
For out-of-order steps, we prove the case when
C=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho_{1}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}}

and
C_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{{\rho}.({e}\rightarrow{e^{\prime}})}}{}_{c}}C_{2}^{\prime}
, with the other cases following a similar argument.
For the step to occur, we must have that
\rho\cap\rho_{1}=\varnothing
and
\rho\cap\operatorname{cloc}({C_{1}})=\varnothing
.
Then for
L\in\rho
, we have that
L\notin\operatorname{cloc}({C_{1}})\cup\rho_{1}
, so
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}
is a value,
and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho_{1}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho_{1}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho_{1}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}let}}~{\rho_{1}.x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}in}}~{C_{2}}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}\mathrel{\fatsemi}\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}{\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{L}}
.
Therefore the conclusion follows directly by induction on
C_{2}
with no change to the context.
∎

Lemma 97 (System Step Rearranging).

If
\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}
,
\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{2}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{2}
, and for every
L
,
\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L}\leq\lfloor{ls_{1}}\rfloor_{L}
,
then there is some
ls_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq ls_{1}
such that

(1)

\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}
,

(2)

for all
L
,
\lfloor{ls_{1}}\rfloor_{L}=\lfloor{ls_{2}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}
, and

(3)

\lvert{ls_{1}}\rvert=\lvert{ls_{2}}\rvert+\lvert{ls_{1}^{\prime}}\rvert
.

That is, if every location has made at least the same steps in the same order in
\Pi_{1}
as in
\Pi_{2}
,
then we can “catch up”
\Pi_{2}
to
\Pi_{1}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
ls_{2}
.
If
ls_{2}=\epsilon
is empty, then the result holds trivially with
ls_{1}^{\prime}=ls_{1}
.
Otherwise, let
ls_{2}=l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{2}^{\prime}
and
\Pi\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{S}}\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{2}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
.
Because there is some
L\in\operatorname{loc}({l})
, and as
\lfloor{l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{2}}\rfloor_{L}\leq\lfloor{ls_{1}}\rfloor_{L}
,
we must have that
L\in\operatorname{loc}({ls_{1}})
, and so
\operatorname{loc}({l})\cap\operatorname{loc}({ls_{1}})\neq\varnothing
.
Thus by Lemma
92
, there is
some
ls_{a},ls_{b}
, and
l^{\prime}
such that
ls_{1}=ls_{a}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptl^{\prime}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{b}
,

\operatorname{loc}({l})\cap\operatorname{loc}({ls_{a}})=\varnothing
,
and either
l^{\prime}=l
, or
l=L.\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e}\Rightarrow{e_{1}}})
and
l^{\prime}=L.\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e}\Rightarrow{e_{2}}})
where
e_{1}\neq e_{2}
.
However, the latter case is impossible because

\displaystyle\lfloor{l+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{2}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e}\Rightarrow{e_{1}}})+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle\leq\lfloor{ls_{1}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{ls_{a}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptl^{\prime}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{b}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({{e}\Rightarrow{e_{2}}})+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{b}}\rfloor_{L}

which implies that
e_{1}=e_{2}
.
Then if the intermediate system states of
ls_{1}
are

\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{a}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{a}\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi^{\prime}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{b}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}
,
by Parallel Confluence, we have that
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{a}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi^{\prime}
.
We then apply the inductive hypothesis to
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{a}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi^{\prime}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{b}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}

and
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{2}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
to yield
ls_{1}^{\prime}
where
\Pi_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}
.
Then
ls_{1}^{\prime}
suffices because

(1)

\Pi_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}
,

(2)

for all
L
, either
L\in\operatorname{loc}({l})
and so
\lfloor{ls_{a}}\rfloor_{L}=\epsilon
,
or
L\notin\operatorname{loc}({l})
and
\lfloor{l}\rfloor_{L}=\epsilon
, so in either case

\displaystyle\lfloor{ls_{1}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{ls_{a}}\rfloor_{L}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{l}\rfloor_{L}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{b}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{l}\rfloor_{L}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{a}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{b}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{l}\rfloor_{L}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{2}^{\prime}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{l}\rfloor_{L}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{2}^{\prime}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}

\displaystyle=\lfloor{ls_{2}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L},

(3)

\lvert{ls_{1}}\rvert=1+\lvert{ls_{a}}\rvert+\lvert{ls_{b}}\rvert=1+\lvert{ls_{2}^{\prime}}\rvert+\lvert{ls_{1}^{\prime}}\rvert=\lvert{ls_{2}}\rvert+\lvert{ls_{1}^{\prime}}\rvert
.

The following diagram summarizes the argument.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{2}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ls_{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ls_{2}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ls_{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ls_{1}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ls_{b}}

∎

Theorem 11 (Augmented Divergence-Weakened Soundness).

If
\,\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
,

\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega
,

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}\precsim\Pi
,
and
\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}
,
then there is some
C^{\prime}
,
\Omega^{\prime}

\Pi_{2}
,
\Pi_{3}
, and
ls^{\prime}
where

(1)

C\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{c}}C^{\prime}
,

(2)

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}\precsim\Pi_{2}
,

(3)

\Pi\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{2}
,

(4)

\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{3}
,

(5)

\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{3}
, and

(6)

for all
C^{\prime\prime}
,
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
and
R
, if
\mathchoice{\left\langle{C^{\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime}}\right\rangle}{\langle{C^{\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime}}\rangle}{\langle{C^{\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime}}\rangle}{\langle{C^{\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime}}\rangle}\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}\mathchoice{\left\langle{C^{\prime\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle}{\langle{C^{\prime\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}\rangle}{\langle{C^{\prime\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}\rangle}{\langle{C^{\prime\prime}},{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}\rangle}
, then
\operatorname{rloc}({R})\cap\operatorname{loc}({ls^{\prime}})=\varnothing
.

That is, any next step that
C^{\prime}
can make involves locations
which did not make any steps from
\Pi_{2}
to
\Pi_{3}
.
The diagram below summarizes the Theorem.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ls_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\precsim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ls^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\precsim}

Proof.

The proof proceeds by strong induction on the length of
ls_{1}
.
In general, we consider two cases.
In the first, suppose that for all
C^{\prime}
and
R
, that
C\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}C^{\prime}
, implies
\operatorname{rloc}({R})\cap\operatorname{loc}({ls_{1}})=\varnothing
.
Then the conclusion follows with
C^{\prime}=C
and
ls^{\prime}=\epsilon
.
Otherwise, we suppose that there is some
C^{\prime}
,
R
, and
L
where
C\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}C^{\prime}
and
L\in\operatorname{rloc}({R})\cap\operatorname{loc}({ls_{1}})
.

We claim that there is some
C^{\prime\prime}
,
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
,
ls_{2}
,
ls_{1}^{\prime}
,
\Pi_{2}
, and
\Pi_{3}
such that

(a)

C\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{c}}C^{\prime\prime}
,

(b)

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}}\precsim\Pi_{2}
,

(c)

\Pi\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{2}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{2}
,

(d)

\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{1}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{3}
,

(e)

ls_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq ls_{2}
, and

(f)

for all
L^{\prime}
,
\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L^{\prime}}\leq\lfloor{ls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L^{\prime}}
.

Indeed, if
R
is a step in a local program
\rho.e
, then this follows by Lemma
95

by stepping to
\rho.e^{\prime}
where
e^{\prime}
is provided by the Lemma,
and otherwise it follows by EPP Completeness and Lemma
93
by choosing
ls_{2}=\mathchoice{\left\llbracket R\right\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}
.
In either case, we can apply Lemma
97
to the result to yield some
ls_{2}^{\prime}\subseteq ls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}

where
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{2}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{3}
,
\lvert{ls_{1}}\rvert+\lvert{ls_{1}^{\prime}}\rvert=\lvert{ls_{2}}\rvert+\lvert{ls_{2}^{\prime}}\rvert
,
and for all
L^{\prime}
,
\lfloor{ls_{1}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L^{\prime}}=\lfloor{ls_{2}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444ptls_{2}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L^{\prime}}
.

We then claim that
\lvert{ls_{1}^{\prime}}\rvert<\lvert{ls_{2}}\rvert
.
Indeed, if we had
\lvert{ls_{1}^{\prime}}\rvert\geq\lvert{ls_{2}}\rvert
,
then because
ls_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq ls_{2}
, we should have that
ls_{1}^{\prime}=ls_{2}
.
However, because
\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L}\leq\lfloor{ls_{1}}\rfloor_{L}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{1}^{\prime}}\rfloor_{L}=\lfloor{ls_{1}}\rfloor_{L}+\kern-5.59721pt+\kern 3.44444pt\lfloor{ls_{2}}\rfloor_{L}
,
this would imply that
\lfloor{ls_{1}}\rfloor_{L}=\epsilon
.
But this is a contradiction, as
L\in\operatorname{loc}({ls_{1}})
and
ls_{1}\neq\epsilon
.

It follows that
\lvert{ls_{2}^{\prime}}\rvert=\lvert{ls_{1}}\rvert+\lvert{ls_{1}^{\prime}}\rvert-\lvert{ls_{2}}\rvert<\lvert{ls_{1}}\rvert
,
so we can apply induction to
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}}\precsim\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls_{2}^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{3}

to yield some
C^{\prime\prime\prime}
,
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}
,
\Pi_{4}
,
\Pi_{5}
, and
ls^{\prime}
satisfying (1–6).
We claim these are satisfactory for the final conclusion, as

(1)

C\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{c}}C^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{c}}C^{\prime\prime\prime}
,

(2)

\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime\prime\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime\prime\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}}}\precsim\Pi_{4}
,

(3)

\Pi\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{2}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{4}
,

(4)

\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{3}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{5}
,

(5)

\Pi_{4}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{5}
, and

(6)

for all
C^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
and
R
, if
C^{\prime\prime\prime}\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}C^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
, then
\operatorname{rloc}({R})\cap\operatorname{loc}({ls^{\prime}})=\varnothing
.

The diagram below summarizes the argument.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{5}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{4}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\prime\prime\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ls_{1}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ls_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ls_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\precsim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ls_{2}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\precsim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ls^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\precsim}

∎

See
6

Proof.

Immediately follows by Theorem
11
and Lemma
35
.
∎

Theorem 12 (Augmented Deadlock Freedom).

If
\,\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}\vdash^{\mkern-3.0mu\scriptscriptstyle+}}C\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}:}\tau\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0,0.3242,0.6641}{\mathrel{\rhd}}}\rho
and

\operatorname{NL}({\rho})\subseteq\Omega
,
then whenever
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega}}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}
,
either
\Pi_{1}
is final or it can step.

Proof.

By Theorem
11
,
there is some
C^{\prime}
,
\Omega^{\prime}
,
\Pi_{2}
,
\Pi_{3}
, and
ls^{\prime}
satisfying (1–6) of that theorem.
If
\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{+}_{S}}\Pi_{3}
, then the conclusion is obviously satisfied as
\Pi_{1}
can step at least once.
Otherwise we suppose that
\Pi_{1}=\Pi_{3}
.
By Type Soundness, either
C^{\prime}
is a value or can step.
If
C^{\prime}
is a value, then because
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\pitchfork}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}\precsim\Pi_{2}
, the system
\Pi_{2}
is final.
But because
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{ls^{\prime}}}{}^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{1}
,
\Pi_{1}
must also be final, as desired.
Otherwise suppose that
C^{\prime}
steps as
C^{\prime}\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}_{c}}C^{\prime\prime}
.
Then by EPP Completeness,

\Pi_{2}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\mathchoice{\left\llbracket R\right\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}}}{}^{+}_{S}}\Pi_{2}^{\prime}
.
Then as
\operatorname{rloc}({R})=\operatorname{loc}({\mathchoice{\left\llbracket R\right\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}})
and
\operatorname{rloc}({R})\cap\operatorname{loc}({ls^{\prime}})
by the conclusion of Lemma
11
,
by Parallel Confluence we can step
\Pi_{1}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\mathchoice{\left\llbracket R\right\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}{\llbracket R\rrbracket}}}{}^{+}_{S}}\Pi_{4}
,
so
\Pi_{1}
must be able to step at least once.
∎

See
7

Proof.

Follows directly from Theorem
12
and Lemma
35
.
∎
